### Page 2

2  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/
上週重要市場資訊
 隨著法國距離下屆總統大選僅剩六個月，極左翼與政府間的政治對立升溫，然而
法國政府急需推動預算案以控制財政赤字與龐大債務。在內政危機加劇與工會
預告 11月大罷工的背景下，市場對法國債務可持續性的擔憂升溫，將帶動法德
公債利差維持高檔，進而推升歐元區主權風險溢酬並拖累歐元表現。
 上週路透最新調查顯示，在 100 位受訪經濟學家中，高達 94%預期聯準會將於
10 月會議維持利率在 3.75%-4.00% 不變，惟全員一致預估 12 月將重啟升息。
儘管美國受 AI 投資熱潮帶動、經濟成長強勁，且美伊戰事推升油價突破百元
大關、加徵關稅使 PCE 通膨至少在 2028 年前難以回到 2% 目標，官員仍傾
向審慎行事。不過逾四成專家警告，聯準會恐落後通膨形勢，後續面臨至少再升
息兩次甚至擴大升息幅度的上行風險，預期美元大幅回檔的空間小，逢低承接買
盤依然強勁。
 路透最新調查顯示，受日圓疲軟與中東戰事推升能源及進口成本、加劇通膨壓力

影響，近七成受訪經濟學家預計日本央行將於 12月再度升息至 1.50%，延續 6
月與 9 月以來的較快緊縮步伐；同時，逾半數專家預估終端利率將上看 2.00%
或更高。不過，高達近四分之三受訪者警告，若高市早苗內閣提名的鴿派委員再
次投下反對票，恐壓抑日圓表現。在歐美央行年底前亦可能升息的背景下，日本
央行維持較快的緊縮節奏，有助於防止美日、歐日利差進一步擴大，避免日圓在
年底遭遇新一輪劇烈拋售，為日圓提供實質的下檔保護。

### Page 3

3  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
 美元兌新台幣匯率走勢  
Oct/27/2026 
＜上週走勢＞ 
    上週台幣匯價高點為 32.360，低點在 32.704，以 32.508作收，較前一周收盤價
貶值 1 角 7 分。 
上週台幣走貶，周初，外資延續前周大幅匯出的走勢，台幣跳空開低，跌破今
年 7 月的低點 32.528；隨著台股表現不佳，外資賣超台股並匯出，台幣續創低位，
最低來到 32.704周內低點；而後台股低檔反彈，外資由匯出轉為雙向進出，加以出
口商積極進場拋售美金，台幣由貶轉升至 32.500下方；臨近周末，市場擔憂颱風來
襲周五可能休市，增添連續假期變數，外資再度大幅賣超台股並匯出，令台幣又跌
回 32.600上方，終場接在央行調節之下收在 32.508。 
＜未來分析＞ 
    預估本週台幣走勢偏弱。近期全球科技股因 AI 類股估值偏高及融資風險疑慮
而持續修正，市場風險偏好降溫，股市面臨修正，外資連續賣超台股並匯出，台幣
走勢持續面臨壓力。另一方面，美伊戰事和談進展的消息反覆，令國際油價節節攀
升，突破每桶 110美元上方，引發通膨升溫的擔憂，支撐利率維持在高檔的預期，
美元指數也在 101.5 上方高位震盪，將不利台幣的表現。盤勢上來看，外資隨著股
市回檔修正表現偏向賣超台股並匯出，台幣偏弱，不過本周適逢月底，預計匯價逢
高仍持續有出口商拋售美金的需求，將限制台幣走貶的幅度。綜合以上，預估本週
台幣走勢偏弱。

＜預估本週＞ 美元兌台幣：32.300-32.800

### Page 4

4  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
美元兌人民幣匯率走勢  
Oct/27/2026 
＜上週走勢＞ 
上週人民幣走升。周初中國公布9月工業生產物價指數 5.5%，表現優於預期的
5.4%(前值 5.2%)，且第三季 GDP表現 4.5%，也優於預期的 4.4%(前值 4.3%)，基本
面穩健支撐人民幣升破 6.6800 整數關口；不過周間美元指數持續於 101.5 上方高位
震盪，加以美伊戰事未見緩解，國際油價持續飆升，突破每桶 110 美元上方，人民
幣於 6.6800下方高檔震盪；臨近周末，人行中間價格連續走升，升破6.7200整數關
口，週五最高來到 6.7180，顯示中國人行允許人民幣溫和升值的走勢，終場境外人
民幣升破 6.6700整數關口 ， 收在6.6690， 週線升值160點 ， 境內人民幣則收在6.6704，
週線升值 166 點。 
＜未來分析＞ 
     預估本週人民幣偏升。就中國國內市場來看，人行中間價持續偏升，將持續帶
動人民幣走升，加以出口商實質拋匯需求將支撐人民幣表現；就國際市場來看，最
新利率期貨顯示 ， 本周聯準會利率決議有9成的機率維持利率在 3.75%~4.00%不變 ，
不過年底前仍有一碼的升息空間，加以地緣政治風險未見緩解，能源價格高檔震盪
推升通膨與利率預期，美元指數維持在 101.5 上方高檔震盪，將限制人民幣升值的
速度跟幅度；最後就技術面來看，USD/CNH日線圖中，各天期均線向下發散，匯價
維持在五日均線之下交投，均線呈現空頭排列，顯示空方勢力較盛，綜合以上，預
估本週人民幣偏升。

＜預估本週＞ 美元兌人民幣：6.6300-6.6900

### Page 5

5  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
美元兌日圓匯率走勢  
         Oct/27/2026

＜上週走勢＞ 
    上週 USD/JPY 震盪下跌。週初，USD/JPY 大致在 158 的整數關卡上下波動；
週間，AI相關概念股遭拋售，加之中東局勢緊張推升避險需求，10年期美債殖利率
跌破 5%，避險資金亦流入日圓，帶動 USD/JPY 下行最低來至 156.67；臨近週末，
市場避險需求持續加之日本 9 月通膨數據高於日本央行 2%目標，USD/JPY 一度下
跌至 156.06，後逢低買盤進場跌幅有所收斂，終場收在 156.86，週線下跌 113點。 
＜未來分析＞ 
  預估本週 USD/JPY區間震盪，主要原因如下： 一、美國10 月 S&P製造業 PMI
為 56.0高於 9 月的 55.9且為 53個月高點，服務業則為 57.9低於 9月的 58.8，綜合
PMI為 57.6亦低於 9 月的 58.4，顯示美國商業活動雖略有放緩但仍處於擴張，經濟
基本面依然穩健將持續支撐美元；此外，中東局勢惡化使能源供應風險上升，布蘭
特原油價格突破 116美元，為六個月以來高點，也將有利USD/JPY上行。二、日本
9 月全國整體消費者物價指數（CPI）年增 2.1%，且 9 月核心 CPI年增 2.1%，為九
個月以來首次超過日本央行 2%的目標，強化市場對日本央行短期升息的預期，預計
為日圓帶來支撐。三、就技術面而言，日線圖中，USD/JPY匯價處於各天期均線之
下且接連跌破 158 及 157 關卡，後在 156 整數關卡暫時獲得支撐，顯示逢低仍有買
盤進場，將限縮下行幅度。綜合以上，預估USD/JPY本週呈現區間震盪的可能性較
高。 
＜預估本週＞ 美元兌日幣：155.00-158.50 
前週交易區間 
156.06 158.59

### Page 6

6  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
美元兌歐元匯率走勢  
         Oct/27/2026

＜上週走勢＞ 
    上週 EUR/USD震盪下跌。週初，EUR/USD在 1.1200整數關卡窄幅波動；週間
受到油價及美債殖利率回落，外加法國社會局勢略見緩解影響，帶動匯價反彈至
1.1260遇到賣壓後回落，後續大致在1.1190至 1.1250間波動；臨近週末，歐元區10
月份 PMI表現低迷，且市場對於法國政局和財政赤字擔憂持續，拖累 EUR/USD轉
若，匯價一度下行來至 1.1183，終場收在 1.1202，週線下跌 37點。 
＜未來分析＞ 
  預估本週 EUR/USD震盪偏空，主要原因如下：一、上週公佈歐元區10月 PMI
顯示德、法表現分化，法國製造業及服務業均表現疲弱低於50榮枯線，拖累歐元區
10 月綜合 PMI 由 9 月 53.1 下滑至 51.9，為四個月以來新低，經濟基本面承壓將不
利 EUR/USD上行；此外，法國社會及政經局勢不穩導致法債殖利率突破5%，市場
對其財政及政治危機擔憂持續，也將不利歐元表現。二、美國上週初請失業金人數
降至 19.4萬人，為三個月以來新低，顯示勞動力市場依然穩健，且10月 S&P製造
業 PMI為 56.0續創 53個月新高，服務業及綜合PMI維持穩健，顯示美國經濟仍具
韌性，預期將限縮 EUR/USD上行空間。三、技術面而言，在日線圖中，EUR/USD
各天期均線維持空頭排列，且匯價持續承壓於月線之下，顯示短線空方趨勢仍未改
變，在能有效突破上方月線前仍以震盪偏空看待。綜合以上，預估 EUR/USD 本週
呈現震盪偏空的可能性較高。 
＜預估本週＞ 歐元兌美元：1.1120-1.1270 
前週交易區間 
1.1180 1.1260

### Page 7

7  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
新台幣利率走勢分析

115.10.19-115.10.22  
銀行隔夜拆款利率 0.805% ~ 0.825%  
30天期短票利率   1.67% ~ 1.68%  
  公債－10年期- A15109 2.000%~2.002% 
      － 5 年期- A15108     1.88%

一、 台幣貨幣市場部分，儘管市場預期強烈颱風「薔琵」來襲，週五金融市場可能
因颱風假而暫停交易，銀行間多預留帳上資金以應不時之需，但市況仍屬充
裕，即便有新增調度需求，也很容易滿足，令短率朝區間低位移動。 
週四隔夜拆款利率區間在 0.805%~0.825%， 30 天期短票主要成交在
1.65%~1.68%，利率低位較上週下跌約 1 個基本點。

二、 本週市場交投冷清，10 年期指標券 A15109 週四於等殖系統成交在 2.002%，
5 年期指標券 A15108則未成交，最近成交利率為 1.88%。 
週二 10 年期公債標售得標利率 2.001%，落於市場預期區間下緣，並創 2008
年以來新高，顯示市場持續反映經濟成長與通膨預期。 
考量通膨前景及國際債市走勢仍具不確定性，市場觀望氣氛濃厚；惟第四季公
債供給相對有限，可望抑制殖利率大幅彈升。預期短線台債殖利率維持區間整
理，5 年券及 10 年券交易區間分別估於 1.82%~1.84%及 1.98%~2.01%。

## Document 094: 2026年9月消費統計

### Page 1

日本

株式会社大和総研 丸の内オフィス 〒100-6756 東京都千代田区丸の内一丁目 9番 1号 グラントウキョウ ノースタワー 
このレポートは投資勧誘を意図して提供するものではありません。このレポートの掲載情報は信頼できると考えられる情報源から作成しておりますが、その正確性、完全
性を保証するものではありません。また、記載された意見や予測等は作成時点のものであり今後予告なく変更されることがあります。㈱大和総研と大和証券㈱は、㈱大和
証券グループ本社を親会社とする大和証券グループの会社です。内容に関する一切の権利は㈱大和総研にあります。無断での複製・転載・転送等はご遠慮ください。

2026年 11月 10日 全9頁

Indicators Update 
2026年 9月消費統計 
サービスを中心に消費全体が弱く、前月から減少

経済調査部 エコノミスト 龐 鈞文

[要約] 
◼ 2026年9月の家計調査における二人以上世帯の実質消費支出は前月比▲2.8％と3カ月
ぶりに減少した。複数の需要側統計を用いて補正した世帯消費動向指数（ CTIミクロ）
で見ても実質消費は同▲1.6％と2カ月連続で減少した。また、供給側統計の1つであ
る商業動態統計では、CPIの財指数で実質化した小売販売額は同▲1.0％だった。総じ
て見れば、9月の個人消費は前月から減少したと判断される。 
◼ 先行きの個人消費は底堅く推移するとみている。名目賃金は最低賃金の引き上げもあ
って上昇が続く見込みである。一方、政府の物価高対策が予定されるものの、原油高
などによる幅広い分野への価格上昇の波及、円安や人件費上昇による価格転嫁などを
通じて個人消費を下押しする可能性には引き続き注意が必要だ。

図表 1：各種消費指標の概況（単位：％）

（注）百貨店売上高、コンビニエンスストア売上高、スーパー売上高の前年比は店舗数調整後。 
（出所）各種統計より大和総研作成

＜2026年9月の消費総括＞サービスを中心に消費全体が弱い 
需要側統計である家計調査における二人以上世帯の実質消費支出は、前月比▲2.8％と3カ月
ぶりに減少した。複数の需要側統計を用いて補正した世帯消費動向指数（CTIミクロ）で見て
も実質消費は同▲1.6％と 2カ月連続で減少した（図表 1）。また、供給側統計の 1つである商
業動態統計では、CPIの財指数で実質化した小売販売額は同▲1.0％だった。総じて見れば、
2026年 9月の個人消費は前月から減少したと判断される。

＜CTIミクロ・家計調査（需要側）＞全ての費目が減少 
2026年 9月の CTIミクロ（二人以上の世帯）を費目別に見ると、10大費目1のうち、「教養娯
楽」（前月比▲3.3％）、「教育」（同▲6.5％）、「食料」（同▲0.9％）、「光熱・水道」（同▲
3.1％）、「家具・家事用品」（同▲ 4.8％）、「交通・通信」（同▲0.8％）、「保健医療」（同
▲2.4％）、「住居」 （同▲1.6％） 、「被服及び履物」 （同▲3.0％） 、「その他」 （同▲0.4％）の全
ての費目が減少した（図表 2）。全ての費目が減少したのは消費税率が 10％に引き上げられた
2019年 10月以来だ。

続いて、CTIミクロの10大費目に含まれる個別品目への支出について確認する。「教養娯楽」
は2カ月連続で減少した。教養娯楽用品や教養娯楽耐久財を中心に支出が減少した。「教育」は
3カ月連続で減少した。授業料等を中心に支出が減少した2。「食料」は3カ月ぶりに減少した。
飲料を中心に、穀類などへの支出が減少した。「光熱・水道」は2カ月ぶりに減少した。電気代
を中心に、上下水道料などへの支出が減少した。「家具・家事用品」は2カ月連続で減少した。
家庭用耐久財を中心に、家事雑貨などへの支出が減少した。「交通・通信」は2カ月連続で減少
した。自動車等関係費や通信を中心に支出が減少した。「保健医療」は3カ月連続で減少した。
保健医療用品・器具を中心に支出が減少した。「住居」は2カ月ぶりに減少した。設備修繕・維
持を中心に支出が減少した。「被服及び履物」は2カ月連続で減少した。シャツ・セーター類や
他の被服を中心に支出が減少した。「その他」は2カ月連続で減少した。諸雑費を中心に支出が
減少した。 
消費支出を財・サービス別に見ると（大和総研による季節調整値、図表3中段右）、サービス
（前月比▲3.9％）が2カ月連続で減少し、全体を押し下げた。非耐久財（同▲1.6％）は2カ月
ぶりに、耐久財（同▲3.6％）は 5カ月連続で、半耐久財（同▲3.0％）は 2カ月連続で、いず
れも減少した。 
家計調査における基礎的支出は前月比▲0.6％と2カ月ぶりに減少した。また、選択的支出は
同▲7.6％と 3カ月ぶりに大幅に減少した（いずれも大和総研による季節調整値、図表 3中段
左）。

2 ただし、授業料等は子どもや学生がいる一部の世帯以外はほとんど支出しないため、サンプルサイズが小さ
く振れが大きい。

＜商業動態統計（供給側）＞小売販売額は名目・実質ともに2カ月連続で減少 
2026年 9月の商業動態統計によると、名目小売販売額は前月比▲0.7％と 2カ月連続で減少
した（図表 4、5） 。また、CPIの財指数で実質化した小売販売額は同▲1.0％だった。 
名目小売販売額を業種別に見ると、7業種中 4 業種が前月から減少した。「その他小売業」
（前月比▲2.1％）、「燃料小売業」 （同▲2.7％）ともに 2カ月連続で減少した。「機械器具小売
業」 （同▲2.3％）も 2カ月連続で減少した。家電大型専門店の販売額（大和総研による季節調
整値） （同▲3.2％）などが減少した。「飲食料品小売業」 （同▲0.2％）は 2カ月連続で減少し
た。コンビニのファーストフード及び日配食品（同▲2.3％）と加工食品（同▲1.9％）が減少
した。 
他方、3業種が前月から増加した。「自動車小売業」 （前月比＋0.1％）は 5カ月ぶりにプラス
に転じた。9月の新車販売台数（大和総研による季節調整値）は同＋2.0％と増加しており3、
この結果とも整合的だ。「織物・衣服・身の回り品小売業」 （同＋4.2％）は2カ月ぶりに増加し
た。気温が低下したこと（巻末図表「全国の平均気温・日照時間・降水量」）や休日数が多か
ったことなどにより、秋物商品の販売が好調 だったとみられる。「各種商品小売業」（同＋
2.1％）は 2カ月ぶりに増加した。スーパーの商品販売額（同＋1.0％）と百貨店の商品販売額
（同＋0.5％）が増加した。

7 /9

＜2026年10月の消費＞9月から減少を見込む 
業界統計や個社データ、JCB消費NOWをもとに判断すると、2026年10月の消費は9月から減
少したとみている4。財消費では、10月前半の実績をもとに試算した家電のJCB消費額（大和総
研による季節調整値） 、コンビニのJCB消費額（同） 、新車販売台数（同）とアパレルの売上高
は減少した。一方、百貨店の売上高は堅調に推移した。サービス消費では、宿泊の JCB消費額
（同）は前月から減少した一方、外食のJCB消費額（同）は増加した。

＜先行き＞個人消費は底堅く推移する見込み／価格上昇の広がりに注意 
先行きの個人消費は底堅く推移するとみている。名目賃金の高い伸びが下支えとなるだろう。
ただし、原油高の波及や円安などによる物価上昇が実質賃金を押し下げ、消費の重荷となる可
能性には注意が必要だ。 
名目賃金の伸びは高水準で推移するだろう。厚生労働省によると、全ての都道府県で 10～12
月に最低賃金が引き上げられる。2026年度の最低賃金の全国加重平均額は1,177円で5、引き上
げ率は＋5.0％（前年度は＋6.3％）と高水準を維持した。また、企業収益の改善などを背景に、
高水準の年末賞与が期待される。 
消費者物価について、12月使用分から 2027年 2月使用分までの電気・ガス料金の補助とい
った政府の物価高対策が実施される予定だ6。ただし、原油高などにより、原材料費や輸送費の
上昇を通じて幅広い分野の価格に波及し、円安や人件費の高騰などによる価格転嫁も物価を押
し上げ、消費を下押しする可能性には引き続き注意が必要だ。また、帝国データバンクによる
と、飲食料品の 9～10月の大規模な値上げラッシュは一服したが、11月の値上げ品目数は前年
11月の約 10倍規模となり、引き続き高水準で推移している。賃上げや物流費、エネルギーコ
ストの上昇など、食品を取り巻くコスト環境が楽観視できる状況にはないため、値上げは続く
とみている7。 
また、金利上昇による住宅ローン等の利払い負担増が個人消費にもたらす悪影響にも警戒す
る必要がある8。さらに、消費動向調査（内閣府）によると、10月の消費者態度指数（二人以
上の世帯、季節調整値）は 35.0（前月差▲0.4pt）と 2カ月連続で小幅に低下し、依然として
低水準にあり（巻末図表「消費者マインド」参照） 、節約志向の高まりに引き続き注意が必要
だ。

4 詳細は、拙稿「消費データブック（2026/11/5号）」（大和総研レポート、2026年11月 5日）を参照。 
5 厚生労働省「全ての都道府県で地域別最低賃金の改定額が答申されました ～答申での全国加重平均額は昨
年度から 56円引上げの1,177円～」（2026年9月3日） 
6 資源エネルギー庁「電気・ガス料金支援」（2026年11月9日閲覧） 
7 帝国データバンク「『食品主要 195社』価格改定動向調査 ― 2026年 11月 飲食料品値上げ、年間 2万品
目超え 11月は 1477品目 秋の『値上げラッシュ』は一服」（2026年10月 30日） 
8 詳細は、神田慶司・畑中宏仁・中村華奈子・横田凱「日本経済見通し：2026年8月」（大和総研レポート、 
2026年8月24日）を参照。

Greater China Technology Hardware
Asia Pacific
Industry View In-Line
(-) PC OEMs and ODMs: AMD (covered by Joseph Moore) mentioned that PC sell-
through slowed down in late 3Q, particularly in the consumer segment, and that it
estimates PC units will fall in double digits (%) in CY27. This aligns with Morgan

Stanley's estimate at a ~11% y/y decline (report link). AMD also mentioned that it
expects to outgrow the market in CY27.
(+) Server OEMs, ODMs, and component makers: AMD expects CPU TAM to reach
US$220bn by 2030, implying a ~50% CAGR. It also mentioned that its Venice CPU
began shipments in 3Q and cloud providers are deploying it in 4Q.
(+) ABF substrates:  AMD said that it has secured the wafers, substrates, advanced
packaging, and memory needed for much higher 2027 growth, and is planning to add

more. It also mentioned that its supply chain is quite "fungible" across its PC and
server business, but overall it still "has more demand than it has supply."

Key Takeaways
We continue to view negative PC comments as bearish for OEMs and ODMs
positioned in the lower-end consumer segment, like Acer and Compal.
Strong  server CPU unit growth from AMD would be positive for Lotes, Wiwynn,
Unimicron, and NYPCB...
...and indirectly benefit GCE, EMC, TUC, and Co-Tech.
We see positive implications for Lotes if AMD takes share in both the PC and

Server segments.

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.

below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

### Page 3

King Yuan Electronics Co (2449.TW)
06 November 2026 Citi Research
3
Near-term weakness already in the price; maintain Buy and lower TP to NT$286
- Despite near-term margin pressure from softer-than-expected quarterly revenue
and rising depreciation cost, electricity and OPEX, management sees GM
expansion into next year supported by stronger utilization, operating leverage, and
favorable AI-related product mix, in particular substantial upside from wafer

sorting. We lower our earnings projection for 2026/2027 /2028 by 6%/17%/15%
reflecting near-term GM shortfall and potential increasing depreciation cost.
Considering remaining strong AI cycle, we keep our Buy rating but lower our DCF-
derived TP to NT$286 given our lower estimates and concerns on recent
macro/policy uncertainty. Our new TP of NT$286 implies 25x/17x of our 2027 /28
EPS estimates.

### Page 6

King Yuan Electronics Co (2449.TW)
06 November 2026 Citi Research
6
King Yuan Electronics Co
Company description
Founded in 1987, King Yuan Electronics Corp. (KYEC) mainly provides testing
services for the back-end process of semiconductor production globally. With
more than 30 years of industry experience, KYEC has built up firm
relationships with several leading global IC design/IDM customers. Its
headquarters and production facilities are both located in Taiwan, with sales

offices in North America, Japan, and Singapore.
Investment strategy
We rate KYEC as Buy. As a key testing service provider for AI GPU and ASIC
products, KYEC is well positioned to benefit from the significant AI growth
opportunity in the years to come. Currently, KYEC is the major final testing
service provider for its major customer's AI GPU, and we expect KYEC to
continue to secure its dominant share in its customer's upcoming AI GPU
product lines due to its specialty in burn-in testing, smooth product delivery

capability, and its strong business partnership with its customer. We are also
positive over the growth outlook of its smartphone business thanks to the
integration of AI functionality, leading to a better replacement cycle. We
believe KYEC's major customer in smartphone will benefit from an improved
product mix, which will result in longer testing times for the flagship SoC.
Valuation
Our target price for KYEC of NT$286 is derived using a DCF methodology.

Using Bloomberg data, we apply a risk-free rate of 4%, a risk premium of 5%,
and a beta of 1.4x for a 10.8% cost of equity. With 20% corporate tax and a
3.0% interest rate on its debt, our WACC for KYEC is 9.7%. We assume
terminal sales growth of 5%. We model its adjusted EBIT margin to gradually
improve from 23% in 2024 to c30% in 2035E. At our target price, the shares
would trade at 2027 /28E P/B of 4x/4x and 2027 /28E P/E of 25x/17x, in line
with its global peers.

Risks
Key downside risks that could prevent KYEC shares from reaching our target
price include: 1) weaker-than-expected end-demand for global
smartphone/consumer electronic products; 2) slower ramp for AI-related
products; 3) slower-than-expected demand recovery in the automotive and
base station sector; and 4) pricing pressure on increasing competition.
Prepared for Kevin Lu

Guide to Citi Research Fundamental Research Investment Ratings:
Citi Research stock recommendations include an investment rating and an optional risk rating to highlight high risk stocks.

Risk rating takes into account both price volatility and fundamental criteria. Stocks will either have no risk rating or a High risk
rating assigned.
Investment Ratings: Citi Research investment ratings are Buy, Neutral and Sell. Our ratings are a function of analyst
expectations of expected total return ("ETR") and risk. ETR is the sum of the forecast price appreciation (or depreciation) plus
the dividend yield for a stock within the next 12 months.  The target price is based on a 12 month time horizon. The Investment

rating definitions are: Buy (1) ETR of 15% or more or 25% or more for High risk stocks; and Sell (3) for negative ETR. Any covered
stock not assigned a Buy or a Sell is a Neutral (2). For stocks rated Neutral (2), if an analyst believes that there are insufficient
valuation drivers and/or investment catalysts to derive a positive or negative investment view, they may elect with the approval
of Citi Research management not to assign a target price and, thus, not derive an ETR. Citi Research may suspend its rating

and target price and assign “Rating Suspended” status for regulatory and/or internal policy reasons. Citi Research may also
suspend its rating and target price and assign "Under Review" status for other exceptional circumstances (e.g. lack of
information critical to the analyst's thesis, trading suspension) affecting the company and/or trading in the company's
securities. In both such situations, the rating and target price will show as “—“ and “-“ respectively in the rating history price

chart. Prior to 11 April 2022 Citi Research assigned “Under Review” status to both situations and prior to 11 Nov 2020 only in
exceptional circumstances. As soon as practically possible, the analyst will publish a note re-establishing a rating and
investment thesis. Investment ratings are determined by the ranges described above at the time of initiation of coverage, a
change in investment and/or risk rating, or a change in target price (subject to limited management discretion). At times, the

expected total returns may fall outside of these ranges because of market price movements and/or other short-term volatility
or trading patterns. Such interim deviations will be permitted but will become subject to review by Research Management.
Your decision to buy or sell a security should be based upon your personal investment objectives and should be made only
after evaluating the stock's expected performance and risk.
Catalyst Watch/Short Term Views (“STV”) Ratings Disclosure:

Catalyst Watch and STV Upside/Downside calls: Citi Research may also include a Catalyst Watch or STV Upside or Downside
call to indicate the analyst expects the share price to rise (fall) in absolute terms over a specified period of 30 or 90 days in

reaction to one or more specific near-term catalysts or events impacting the company or the market. A Catalyst Watch will be
published when Analyst confidence is high that an impact to share price will occur; it will be a STV when confidence level is
moderate. A Catalyst Watch or STV Upside/Downside call will automatically expire at the end of the specified 30/90 day
period. The Catalyst Watch will also be automatically removed if share price performance (calculated at market close) exceeds

our ratings distribution table for our Catalyst Watch/STV Upside/Downside rating system. However, we reiterate that we do
not consider No View to be a recommendation. For all Catalyst Watch/STV Upside/Downside calls, risk exists that the
catalyst(s) and associated share-price movement will not materialize as expected.

啟碁(6285.TT) 
產業：電子–通信網路 
無法及時反映原物料漲價，3Q26 毛利率承壓 
 投資評價與建議 
受惠Wi-Fi 7與5G FWA滲透率提升且低軌衛星UT與800G交換器逐步放量，預
估2026/2027 年稅後EPS為13.25/14.23元，過往啟碁forward PE區間落在
15~20倍 ， 考量原物料成本上漲壓抑毛利率 ， 基於2027年獲利 ， 給予15倍PER 。 
 重點評論分析 
無法及時反映原物料漲價，3Q26毛利率承壓，4Q26持續受到影響：3Q26營
收季增23.5%至487億元，係因北美客戶拉貨需求強勁，毛利率季減0.77ppts
至12.2% ， 係因3Q26調整售價僅單純反映原物料漲價成本 ， 並非等比例調整售
價 ， 導致毛利率承壓 ， 目前公司目標將毛利率維持在12%以上之水準 ， 稅後EPS
為4.35元 。 預估4Q26營收季增3.1%至501.8億元 ， 由於目前原物料漲價品項多
且公司與客戶間多有預定合約價格，故無法及時反映漲價，若能漲價亦僅轉嫁
成本，獲利空間遭壓縮，故下修毛利率至12.1%，稅後EPS為3.15元。 
網通規格升級為主要獲利引擎：展望2026~2027年，主要營收動能來自Wi-Fi 
7與5G FWA網通規格升級以及美系低軌衛星客戶終端裝置部署量持續提升。
啟碁目前交換器業務主要透過Tier 1 Switch供應商夥伴間接出貨予CSP客戶，
下世代1.6T規格以及OCS預於2027年送樣 ，目標2028年開始貢獻營收。 4Q26
為是否於美國設廠之關鍵期，公司將視FCC、USMCA與關稅政策等以及客戶
需求，針對涉及資安和國安相關列管之產品線遷至美國生產。 
 投資風險 
原物料漲價與缺料、低軌衛星產品出現競爭者。

2

| 營運概況  
無法及時反映原物料漲價，3Q26毛利率承壓，4Q26持續受到影響：3Q26營收季增23.5%至487億元，主要受惠北美洲
Service Provider與Enterprise客戶拉貨需求強勁，毛利率季減0.77ppts至12.2%，係因3Q26調 整售價僅單純反映原物
料漲價成本，並非等比例調整售價，導致毛利率承壓，目前公司目標將毛利率維持在12%以上之水準，稅後EPS為4.35
元。隨Enterprise、Service Provider與Automotive三大事業群持續開拓新產品、專案與客戶，預估4Q26營收季增3.1%
至501.8億元，由於目前原物料漲價品項多，從Memory擴及PCB板、MLCC、成熟製程IC等零組件，且公司與客戶間多
有預定合約價格，故無法及時反映漲價，若能漲價亦僅轉嫁成本，獲利空間遭壓縮，故下修毛利率至12.1%，公司表示
4Q26至2027全年將持續受到缺料與漲價影響，達供需平衡仍需時間，稅後EPS為3.15元。 
網通規格升級為主要獲利引擎，800G Switch與低軌衛星產品挹注營收動能：展望2026~2027年，啟碁主要營收動能來
自歐美Service Provider與Enterprise客戶之Wi-Fi 7與5G FWA網通規格升級以及美系低軌衛星客戶 終端裝置部署量持
續提升。企業交換器業務方面，啟碁目前沒有計劃供應Data Center Switch予CSP，目前業務模式僅是透過Tier 1 Switch
供應商夥伴間接出貨予CSP客戶，主要原因為公司自身之資源集中於Campus Switch以及Neo-Cloud市場，目前已取得
相當不錯之進展與成果，下世代1.6T規格Switch以及Optical Circuit Switch(OCS)預計於2027年開始送樣，目標於2028
年開始貢獻營收，公司預估Switch業務在未來2至3年內可實現70~90%之年成⻑率。產能方面，目前公司主要生產基地
位於越南廠，產能比重達約6成，近期公司表示2026年累計之資本支出達30億元，主要用於越南擴廠和美國建廠需求，
4Q26為美國設廠關鍵期，是否設廠將視FCC、USMCA、關稅政策等政策以及客戶需求，針對涉及資安和國安相關列管
之產品線遷至美國生產，初期階段先從SKD半散裝料開始，穩定後推進至CKD全散裝料生產，未來不排除將電信運營商
相關製造服務移至美國據點。綜上所述，預估2026/2027年營收年增51.9%/24.3%至1,674.7/2,082.1億元，稅後EPS為
13.25/14.23元。

## Document 329: Cliff Notes_ whiplash (2026-11-12)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Cliff Notes_ whiplash (2026-11-12).pdf
Report source: Westpac IQ
Date: 2026-11-12
Pages: 11
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

#cliffnotes #rba #australia #inflation #us #fomc #ecb #aud #usd
Cliff Notes: whiplash
By Ryan Wells & Elliot Clarke
11:20 November 06 2026 Share
Key insights from the week that was.
In Australia, the RBA delivered another 25bp rate increase at its November meeting, taking the cash
rate to 4.85%. The move was widely expected, reflecting the Board’s continued concern around
inflation following the recent run higher in global energy prices, firmer domestic capacity pressures and

signs that inflation expectations have edged up. Inflation is now not expected to return to the mid-point
of the target band until mid-2028. This is despite weaker growth expectations and the unemployment

### Page 2

rate trending up to 5.1% by end-2028 on the RBA’s forecasts.
Importantly, though, the decision was not unanimous, with three members preferring to remain on hold
given downside risks to activity and the restraining influence of slower growth. Governor Bullock’s
suggestion that it was time to “take stock” after five rate increases also struck a less hawkish note,
while the statement dropped September’s explicit reference to raising rates further “if needed”. In our

view, February represents the next plausible opportunity for another hike, but we see this only as a risk.
Policy’s effect on housing is becoming increasingly visible. Cotality home prices fell a further 1.2% in
October to now be 6.7% below March’s peak. The cumulative price decline is approaching the scale of
previous housing corrections but appears to have regained momentum after September’s rate
increase. Dwelling approvals saw a 5.1% headline gain in September, but this was driven by volatile

unit approvals while detached house approvals fell sharply, suggesting higher rates and the
established-market downturn are beginning to weigh on the construction pipeline.
Official data on nominal household spending meanwhile rose 0.6% in September and 2.1% for Q3;
however, much of the recent acceleration looks to be price-driven, particularly for fuel. Real spending
volumes expanded by a much more modest (but still solid) 0.7% in Q3.
The Westpac-DataX Consumer Panel provides a granular look at income, spending and saving flows.

The latest update suggests that, beneath the resilient headline story, households are increasingly being
squeezed, particularly those with low savings buffers. With another rate increase now delivered,
housing wealth declining and sentiment weak, Q3 increasingly looks like the high-water mark for
growth in consumer spending.
Lastly for Australia, in September the goods trade surplus widened sharply to $2.8bn, with non-
monetary gold trade yet again adding considerable noise month-to-month. The most notable

development was another exceptionally strong result for data-processing equipment imports, taking the
Q3 total to $11.1bn. This is another clear marker of the scale of Australia’s data-centre investment
boom, which should feature prominently in both business investment and imports in the Q3 National
Accounts.
Offshore, the world has been focused on the US mid-term elections. In the House, Senate and a series
of State Governor races, the Democrats experienced resounding victory. Come 2027, the Democrats

will have a comfortable majority in both houses of Congress and are expected to make use of this
strength to respond to voter concerns, particularly over cost-of-living pressures. The election result also
limits President Trump’s ability to fund an extended military action against Iran. Notably, both
immediately before and after the election, President Trump and the administration have exhibited a
greater interest in furthering negotiations with Iran. It is therefore not a surprise that Brent oil has

backed off its most recent highs around USD118 last Friday, although the scale of the drop to near
USD93 yesterday is arguably too optimistic. Brent has since traded back up to above USD99, still 16%
below Friday’s peak.
While the initial reaction to the US election result was a robust broad-based rally in major equity
indexes, concerns over the potential for tighter regulation of AI and the related infrastructure buildout

### Page 3

Related articles
jolted US equities lower overnight – the Nasdaq falling 5.5% and S&P500 4.4%, both a multiple of the
prior session’s gains. That said, US term interest rates and FOMC rate expectations have also been
scaled back; so, while tech may come under greater scrutiny, a foundation for better outcomes across
the rest of the US economy may become evident in time. US data out this week continued to suggest
their economy is in robust health overall. And, if tensions in the Middle East abate, the FOMC’s

concerns over US inflation should subside. Gaining enough comfort for the FOMC to not hike in
December is a high bar, but the probability of further hikes in 2027 should continue to fall.
Turning then to the UK, the Bank of England raised Bank Rate by 25bps to 4.00% at their November
meeting as expected. On the Bank's baseline expectation, UK inflation is forecast to rise from 3.5%yr
at September to 5.00% by mid-2027. Activity data has also surprised to the upside in recent months,

though "there remains limited evidence of knock-on effects on other prices and wages".
In the press conference, Governor Bailey recognised the nascent improvement in energy price
pressures over the past 48 hours and the effectiveness of monetary policy but also conveyed that
"Unless progress towards resolving the conflict is made, or there is other news which softens the risk to
inflationary pressures, a further increase in Bank Rate may be warranted". Governor Bailey's tone and

comments made in the minutes by other Committee members did not suggest this was a decision that
needs to be made in December, but rather a risk to be judged as additional information is received.
This points to further tightening being more likely in early 2027 than before, and only if conditions / risks
warrant.

## Document 333: CoreWeave大涨给AI云“喘息之机”，接下来就看Anthropic IPO和英伟达了

Source PDF: all report pdf/CoreWeave大涨给AI云“喘息之机”，接下来就看Anthropic IPO和英伟达了.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-11-12
Pages: 6
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

CoreWeave 三季报营收与指引双双超预期，单⽇股价暴涨 25% ，带动新云板块飙升逾
12% 。 Anthropic 传出 1.5 万亿美元 IPO 估值、 OpenAI 单季营收⾸破百亿美元，三重利好共
振，提振了此前深陷 AI 回报疑虑的算⼒产业链。下周英伟达财报与 Anthropic 上市，将是这
轮反弹能否⾛远的真正试⾦⽯。
摘要：
CoreWeave 周⼆发布三季度财报，营收和亏损均好于预期，全年指引亦超出市场预期上限。消
息提振了此前因 AI 投资回报担忧⽽持续承压的 “ 新云 ” （ neocloud ）板块。
在 CoreWeave 股价单⽇⼤涨约 25% 的带动下，新云（ NeoCloud ）板块盘中涨幅⼀度超过 12% ，
纳指也收复了中期选举后的失地。市场的乐观情绪还受到两则消息强化： Anthropic 据报正讨论
⾼达 1.5 万亿美元以上的 IPO 估值，打消了此前公司估值将⼤幅下调的担忧；此外， OpenAI 单季
营收强劲、⾸次达到 100 亿美元。

⽽对于即将在下周登陆资本市场的 Anthropic ⽽⾔，这份财报来得恰逢其时 ——CoreWeave 是
Anthropic 的重要算⼒供应商，两家公司的命运在⼀定程度上相互绑定。与此同时，英伟达下周
的财报将是另⼀块试⾦⽯，投资者届时将寻找更多理由判断 AI 基础设施热潮能否持续。
不过，这轮反弹的基础仍不牢固：⼀⽅⾯，资⾦正从超⼤规模云⼚商转向 “ 卖铲⼈ ” 和算⼒租赁
商；另⼀⽅⾯， CoreWeave 未来的融资缺⼝、债务成本及算⼒供给扩张，仍是决定这轮 AI 基建
交易能否⾛远的核⼼变量。
CoreWeave 带动新云板块集体反弹
CoreWeave 三季度营收 36.6 亿美元，超出分析师预期；每股亏损 1.27 美元，亏损幅度⼩于市场
此前担忧的⽔平。更受关注的是全年指引：公司预计 2026 年全年营收在 130 亿⾄ 133 亿美元之
间，⾼于市场⼀致预期上限约 3.4% 。
CoreWeave CEO Michael Intrator 在与分析师的电话会议上透露了⼀个值得关注的细节：今年

新增客⼾中，超过⼀半来⾃ AI 领域以外。这⼀数据在⼀定程度上回应了市场对 AI 算⼒需求过度集
中、⼀旦 AI 投资热潮退却便将⾯临需求断崖的担忧。 Intrator 的表述暗⽰，对⾼性能算⼒的需求
正在向更⼴泛的⾏业渗透，⽽不仅仅依赖 AI 实验室的采购。
在此之前， CoreWeave 股价年内已累计下跌 39% ，是 AI 算⼒板块中跌幅最深的个股之⼀。德意
志银⾏分析师 Brad Zelnick 在财报发布后表⽰，感谢公司提供了 “ 令⼈宽慰的细节 ” ，这句话颇能
代表市场整体情绪。
财报公布后， CoreWeave 股价单⽇⼤涨约 25% ，成为带动新云板块反弹的核⼼标的。
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⻅闻历 搜索

### Page 2

市场数据显⽰，新云概念股当⽇整体上涨逾 12%，⽽⾼盛⼴义 AI 股票篮⼦上涨约 5% ，显著跑赢
⾮ AI 股票。此前受 AI 资本开⽀回报疑虑拖累的算⼒产业链，迎来了⼀轮快速估值修复。
纳指也收复了中期选举后的失地。

### Page 3

但从市场交易结构看，资⾦的选择也反映出投资者尚未完全消除疑虑。算⼒租赁商、半导体企
业等 AI 资本开⽀的 “ 收款⽅ ” 表现更强；微软、⾕歌、亚⻢逊等承担巨额数据中⼼投资的超⼤规模
云⼚商则相对落后。换⾔之，市场眼下更愿意押注 AI 建设周期的直接受益者，⽽⾮继续为巨额资
本开⽀买单的 “ 付款⽅ ” 。
CoreWeave 与 Anthropic ：命运相连
⽽ CoreWeave 的财报对 Anthropic ⽽⾔具有直接意义。
Anthropic 是 CoreWeave 的客⼾，使⽤ CoreWeave 及其他多家新云服务商的算⼒来⽀撑其
Claude 系列产品，同时也与⾕歌等超⼤规模云⼚商签有合同。两家公司从未公开披露合同条
款，但 Intrator 今年 4 ⽉曾将与 Anthropic 的合作描述为 “ 数⼗亿美元级别的合同 ” 。
据彭博报道， Anthropic 计划于下周完成上市。作为⾸家寻求公开上市的美国前沿 AI 实验室，
Anthropic 没有搜索引擎、社交媒体或云业务等传统业务作为 “ 护城河 ” ，其估值将完全取决于市场

对 AI 前景的判断。 CoreWeave 的强劲表现，理论上有助于 Anthropic 在路演中获得更⾼的估值认
可。
最新市场消息进⼀步抬⾼了这场 IPO 的关注度：据报道， Anthropic 的承销商正评估 1.5 万亿美元
或更⾼的 IPO 估值可能性。若该估值预期得以维持， Anthropic 将不只是检验前沿⼤模型公司商
业化能⼒的样本，也将成为市场为 AI ⻓期增⻓故事愿意⽀付何种溢价的直接标尺。此前有消息称
Anthropic 估值可能⼤打折扣，或在 1.5 万亿美元以下。
与此同时， OpenAI 单季营收⾸次达到 100 亿美元的消息，也为 AI 应⽤层需求提供了新的⽀撑。
对投资者⽽⾔，这意味着市场正在尝试寻找 “ 模型收⼊增⻓ — 算⼒采购扩张 — 芯⽚及数据中⼼投
资回报 ” 之间能够相互验证的闭环；但这⼀闭环是否⾜以⽀撑⾼估值，仍有待更多公司财报和订
单数据验证。
对 Anthropic ⽽⾔， CoreWeave 财报释放的积极信号在于：其上游算⼒供应商仍能获得强劲订

单、提⾼收⼊指引并维持融资能⼒。但这也凸显了双⽅的共同⻛险 —— ⼤模型公司对外部算⼒
的需求能否持续⾼增⻓，以及算⼒供应商能否在⾼资本开⽀和⾼负债模式下保持稳定交付，将
共同影响这条产业链的估值基础。值得注意的是， Anthropic 在算⼒供应上还依赖其他⼏家前景
尚不明朗的新云服务商，其中包括 Nscale—— 据报道该公司正在考虑推迟⾃⾝的 IPO 计划。
AI 下⼀个考验：英伟达财报

### Page 4

下周，英伟达将发布财报。如果投资者能够暂时搁置对 CEO ⻩仁勋及其他相关问题的担忧，英
伟达的业绩或将为市场提供更多乐观理由。
英伟达财报的重要性，已不⽌于验证 GPU 需求是否旺盛。市场更希望从中确认三件事：云⼚商
和新云服务商的采购强度是否持续、 Blackwell 等新产品的供给与交付能否匹配需求，以及客⼾
资本开⽀的快速增⻓能否转化为更可持续的收⼊和利润。
本轮反弹中，半导体板块表现强劲，费城半导体指数录得数⽉来最⼤单⽇涨幅，并收复此前⼀
周回调的⼤部分失地；英伟达股价也出现反弹。不过，交易员观察到，部分活跃资⾦和散⼾在
英伟达财报前仍有卖出⾏为。这意味着市场对芯⽚⻰头的预期依旧很⾼，财报若⽆法提供⾜够
强的指引， AI 交易的波动可能再度放⼤。
彭博专栏作家 Dave Lee 分析，早在 2025 年 3 ⽉ CoreWeave 上市之际，他便曾警告这家公司将成
为 “AI ⾰命的第⼀只真正的⻛向标股票 ” ，事实证明确实如此 ——CoreWeave 的股价已成为市场波
动的主要 “ 出⽓筒 ” 。

反弹背后：融资缺⼝与供给扩张仍是硬约束
不过，这轮反弹的基础仍不牢固。 CoreWeave 未来的融资缺⼝、债务成本及算⼒供给扩张，仍
是决定这轮 AI 基建交易能否⾛远的核⼼变量。
CoreWeave 的业绩缓解了 “AI 建设会否因融资受阻⽽戛然⽽⽌ ” 的短期担忧，但并未彻底回答⻓
期问题。
多头逻辑是，CoreWeave 可以围绕客⼾合同设⽴融资主体，以⾼⽐例担保债务覆盖建设成本；
在合同期内偿还债务后，公司仍保留 GPU 资产，并可将已还清融资的设备再次出租。若算⼒⻓
期紧缺，这种模式有望令存量设备持续产⽣现⾦流。
空头则聚焦三个⻛险。⾸先是定价：管理层所提及的每兆⽡收⼊⽔平，与财务报表所反映的实
际变现⽔平之间仍存在差距。其次是融资：市场观点认为，公司明年或⾯临约 330 亿美元的资⾦
缺⼝，仍需依赖新增债务或客⼾预付款填补。最后是供给：到 2028 年前，市场预计将有约 55 吉
⽡新增算⼒容量投放，若供给释放快于需求增⻓，⽼⼀代 GPU 的租⾦和残值都可能承压。
因此， CoreWeave 本季证明的是，在算⼒依然紧张时，公司能够拿到订单并获得融资；但它尚

⽆法证明，供给紧张会持续到⾜以覆盖巨额债务、并⽀撑旧 GPU 残值的阶段。接下来，市场将
密切关注其投资级担保贷款的最终定价、客⼾预付款规模，以及公司资本开⽀和⾃由现⾦流拐
点能否如预期出现。

### Page 5

⻛险提⽰及免责条款
市场有⻛险，投资需谨慎。本⽂不构成个⼈投资建议，也未考虑到个别⽤⼾特殊的投资⽬标、财务状况或需要。⽤⼾应考虑本⽂
中的任何意⻅、观点或结论是否符合其特定状况。据此投资，责任⾃负。
宏观环境并未放⾏，监管不确定性仍是隐患
在美国中期选举后，⺠主党控制国会，德克萨斯州候任州⻓ Gina Hinojosa 在竞选期间曾主张对
数据中⼼建设实施暂停令。 Intrator 上周已致信 Hinojosa ，希望就已获批项⽬的 “ 负责任完⼯ ” 以及
未来项⽬的 “ 持久标准 ” 寻求共识。
在财报电话会议上， Intrator 试图稳定投资者情绪。他表⽰： “ 我们预计不会受到近期重⼤监管变
化的影响。我们认为，这些变化是关于如何建设的表态，⽽⾮是否建设。 ”
除监管外，利率仍可能是 AI 基础设施估值的另⼀重约束。在本轮股市反弹期间，市场对未来加
息的定价有所上升， 10 年期美债期货隐含收益率重新逼近 5% 。对于依赖债务融资建设数据中⼼
的新云公司⽽⾔，融资成本上升会直接影响项⽬回报率和股权估值。

更重要的是，股票、信⽤、利率和原油市场的波动率尚未完全回落。此前建⽴的避险仓位虽在
本轮反弹中部分平仓， VIX ⼀度回落⾄ 17 附近，但跨资产波动仍处于相对偏⾼⽔平。这意味着，
CoreWeave 带来的 “ 喘息之机 ” 能否演变成趋势性修复，仍取决于后续财报、融资市场和宏观利
率环境能否同时给出积极答案。
总体⽽⾔， CoreWeave 财报暂时修复了市场对 AI 算⼒需求和融资能⼒的信⼼，也为 Anthropic
IPO 及英伟达财报营造了更有利的情绪窗⼝。但从 “ 短期空头回补 ” ⾛向 “ ⻓期基本⾯重估 ” ，仍需
回答融资、供给、 GPU 残值和利率四道难题。下周的 Anthropic 与英伟达，将是这轮 AI 反弹能否
延续的两场关键考试。

### Page 6

## Document 336: CoreWeave电话会：拒绝无担保发债，算力需求推动大幅提价，2028年实现盈利

Source PDF: all report pdf/CoreWeave电话会：拒绝无担保发债，算力需求推动大幅提价，2028年实现盈利.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-11-11
Pages: 15
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

CoreWeave 管理层表⽰，算⼒资源 2027 年前已基本售罄， 2028 年需求远超供给。⻓期合
同每兆⽡达 2000-2500 万美元，短期合同超 4000 万美元。融资策略上，公司以客⼾预付款
替代⽆担保债务，未来三个⽉将收取 90 亿美元预付款。随着早期 GPU 折旧到期， 2027-
2028 年续约将带来近百亿美元增量收⼊，预计 2028 年上半年实现盈利转正。此外选后监管
变化暂不影响交付。
摘要：
AI 云计算基础设施提供商 CoreWeave 在最新财报电话会上传递出强烈的供需信号：公司算⼒资
源在 2027 年前已基本售罄， 2028 年需求更是远超可⽤容量数倍，同时公司宣布⼤幅上调旗下服
务价格，并表⽰将以客⼾预付款取代⽆担保债务作为主要融资⼿段。
华尔街⻅闻提及， 11 ⽉ 10 ⽇周⼆美股盘后， CoreWeave 公布第三季度营收 36.61 亿美元，同⽐
增⻓ 168% ，超出分析师预期，并将 2026 年全年收⼊指引上调⾄ 130 亿⾄ 133 亿美元。

电话会上， CoreWeave ⾸席执⾏官 Michael Intrator 表⽰，季度内完成了多项关键⾥程碑：运营
利润率从⼆季度的 5% 跃升⾄ 10% ，提前⼀个季度达成年初既定⽬标；收⼊积压订单（ backlog ）
增⾄约 1330 亿美元，同⽐增⻓ 138% ；活跃算⼒规模扩张⾄逾 1.7 吉⽡，较 2025 年底翻倍。
在资本结构层⾯，CoreWeave 明确表⽰，未来不会在当前收益率⽔平下依赖⽆担保债务市场为
业务融资，也⽆此必要。公司认为，客⼾预付款在条件上更具吸引⼒，可作为替代性融资来
源。
公司进⼀步披露，到 2027 年，已承诺资本⽀出中逾 75% 与投资级客⼾及企业级客⼾相关，显⽰
收⼊质量和履约确定性较⾼。
公司表⽰，未来三个⽉将收取 90 亿美元客⼾预付款， 70% 的交易已收到预付款，当前资⾦安排
对公司⽽⾔⽐⽆担保债务市场更具吸引⼒。同时，公司预计未来数周将发布下⼀款延迟提款定
期贷款（ DDTL ）。
管理层预计， 2027 年全年调整后净亏损将⼤幅收窄，并将在 2028 年上半年实现调整后净利润转

⻓，利润率提升速度超出计划，客⼾需求依然极为强劲，我们最新签订的合同是有史以来盈利能

⼒最强的⼀批。我们最早部署的云基础设施正在以溢价续签合同，产⽣的现⾦流超出了我们当初
进⾏早期投资时的预期。我们的资⾦来源以有担保贷款、客⼾预付款和运营现⾦流为⽀撑。这⼀
系列势头使我们的业务保持韧性，并在当前环境中具备良好的应对能⼒。
以下是具体数据。营收达到 37 亿美元，同⽐增⻓ 168% ，⾼于我们指引区间的上限，环⽐增加超
过 10 亿美元。调整后运营收⼊达到 3.79 亿美元，远⾼于我们 2 亿⾄ 2.6 亿美元的指引，利润率提
前⼀个季度进⼊低两位数区间。营收积压增⻓⾄约 1,330 亿美元，活跃电⼒容量增⾄超过 1.7 吉
⽡，⾃年初以来我们的活跃基础设施规模翻了⼀番。
上个季度，我们谈到了随着基础设施达到规模化，以及我们在⻓短期合同之间实现业务多元化，
经营杠杆将逐步显现。本季度，这⼀进展已清晰体现在我们的业绩和所提供的指引中。我们也谈
到了 AI 将成为各⾏各业组织⼯作⽅式⼀部分的坚定信念，以及构建这⼀未来的⼈们将需要⼀种不
同类型的云。这种信念每个季度都在变得更加具体。

我们的客⼾正在利⽤ AI 推进⽣物学研究、⽀持患者护理、开发⾃主系统，并帮助⼈们学习。我们
的 AI 云平台赋予他们追求这些⽬标的能⼒，并将有价值的应⽤带⼊⽇常⽣活。⽀撑这些有潜⼒的

### Page 5

想法，需要借助可靠算⼒以极具吸引⼒的经济条件构建和部署 AI 的⼯具。我们的职责就是提供这
⼀基础，并为我们的资本赚取丰厚回报。
鉴于近期资本市场的波动，我想阐明这些要素如何相互配合。我们为客⼾创造的价值，赢得了他
们对 CoreWeave 的⻓期承诺。这些承诺是我们融资策略的基础，⽽有纪律的投资使我们能够持
续扩展客⼾所依赖的平台，同时为股东创造复利价值。这⼀切的核⼼是⼀个基本原则：在每⼀个
决策中，我们都寻求最优的经济选择 —— 如何融资、收取多少费⽤、以多快的速度建设。
在超⼤规模层⾯构建 AI 云需要⼤量资本和严格的执⾏⼒。我们在 CoreWeave 成⽴之初就认识到
这⼀点，并围绕服务于这些资本的⻓期客⼾合同来设计我们的商业模式和融资策略。这⼀策略使
我们能够评估每⼀种新的资本来源，并根据市场条件选择能够优化回报的机会。
截⾄第三季度末，我们整个业务的加权平均债务成本为 7.9% ，同⽐下降 250 个基点。展望未来，
我们不会以当前收益率使⽤⽆担保债务市场为我们的业务融资，也⽆需如此。与我们⼀贯的做法

⼀致，我们预计将通过与特定客⼾合同匹配的延迟提款定期贷款（ DDTL ），为交付积压订单所
需的⼤部分剩余资本⽀出提供融资。
2027 年之前，超过 75% 的承诺资本⽀出与投资级及企业客⼾挂钩，其余部分则与全球领先的 AI
实验室和 AI 原⽣公司相关。这些融资⼯具的定价反映了底层客⼾承诺及我们经过验证的交付能
⼒，可为相关资本⽀出提供 70% ⾄ 100% 的融资覆盖。⾃我们 IPO 以来，这些⼯具的定价区间为
SOFR 利率以上 225 ⾄ 550 个基点，⾼质量的投资级客⼾位于区间收窄端， AI 实验室客⼾位于区
间宽松端。按当前利率计算，最⾼端的收益率不⾜ 10% 。
这些融资⼯具使我们能够在设备采购和交付时提取资⾦。相关合同产⽣的现⾦流在合同期限内⽀
付利息并偿还本⾦，同时为 CoreWeave 创造额外现⾦流。按计划，我们已通过这⼀机制偿还了
超过 40 亿美元的债务，其中包括偿还了我们前两笔延迟提款定期贷款（也是我们成本最⾼的融
资⼯具）近⼀半的⾦额。
对于为⻓期承诺提供资⾦的融资⼯具，当客⼾合同结束时，债务将得到偿还，基础设施将由

CoreWeave ⽆负担地完全拥有。这些已偿清的基础设施本⾝正成为⼀个重要的资本来源。每年
有更多基础设施结束合同期，⽆需新投资即可产⽣收⼊，同时设备已购置、数据中⼼已建成，这
降低了我们增⻓过程中的资本密集度。
我们还有其他⼏种重要的资本来源，包括预付款。我们以经济效益为导向评估预付款，权衡其对
定价的影响与融资成本。过去，这使我们转向⽆担保债务市场。⽽在当前环境下，预付款更具吸
引⼒。今年，我们在 70% 的客⼾交易中收取了预付款，部分客⼾预先⽀付了⾼达 100% 的合同⾦
额。仅在未来三个⽉，我们就将从已签署的客⼾合同中收取约 90 亿美元的预付款和定⾦，其中
包括超过 30 亿美元与现有云基础设施续约相关的款项，这些续约价格较原始合同有显著溢价。
我们还预计， 2027 年扣除利息后的 EBITDA （不含预付款带来的收益）将翻⼀番以上，进⼀步增
强我们内部满⾜资本需求的能⼒。此外， OEM 融资⽀持额外的部署，尤其是作为为短期机会服
务的容量提供融资的⾼效⼿段。综合来看，我们预计这些来源将覆盖 2027 年之前绝⼤部分的资

本需求，仅需在⺟公司层⾯筹集有限的资⾦。
我们的⽀出纪律与资本获取能⼒同等重要。在已承诺合同之外，我们基于成效的资本⽀出模式使
我们能够根据 CoreWeave Cloud 的客⼾需求、供应约束（包括监管影响）和资本市场状况，灵
活调整投资节奏。这是⼀种⻛险缓释机制：我们仅在合同和基础设施到位后，才会承诺⼤部分资
本。 Nitin 稍后将对此进⾏更详细的说明。
现在，让我将这⼀融资模式与您在我们业绩中看到的经济效益联系起来。随着更多已签约基础设
施投⼊使⽤，营收在已有投资的基础上持续增⻓，每⼀个新部署在更⼤体量的业务中所占的⽐重
也越来越⼩ —— 这正是我们上季度描述的经营杠杆。调整后运营利润率在第⼀季度约为 1% ，第
⼆季度为 5% ，第三季度超过 10% 。

### Page 6

新业务的经济效益也在持续改善。 7 ⽉份，我们将各 SKU 的定价提⾼了约 25% ，随后在当季晚些
时候再次上调 10% 。进⼊第四季度，定价持续⾛⾼，涨幅已超过成本增加。我们也将更多容量分
配⾄定价合理的短期机会，以每年每兆⽡超过 4,000 万美元的价格出售容量；与此同时，四⾄五
年期⻓期合同的新基础设施定价正升⾄每兆⽡ 2,000 万⾄ 2,500 万美元。⻓期承诺提供了⽀撑我
们建设扩张的可⻅性，短期合同则使我们能够把握强劲的定价环境并赚取溢价回报。我们逐合同
地权衡这种取舍，全⾯评估每项投资的经济效益 —— 它能产⽣的收⼊、运营成本和融资成本。强
劲的需求赋予我们选择如何创造持久价值的主动权。
我们在上⼀代机群中也看到了同样的强劲势头。第三季度， H100 及 Hopper 架构 GPU 的定价环⽐
上涨 30% ⾄ 50% ，且价格⾼于其原始合同。不同⼯作负载对性能和成本组合有不同要求，早期架
构在可靠的规模化云环境中仍具有极具吸引⼒的经济效益。我们正在签署超出 GPU 折旧年限的

多年期承诺。随着价格和运营成本固定⽽折旧逐步消散，这些基础设施在合同后期的利润率将进
⼀步提升。
2027 年将有超过 13 万枚 GPU 结束合同， 2028 年将再有约 17 万枚 GPU 到期。我们已有⼤量客⼾
在争夺这些容量。按当前定价⽔平， 2027 年的续约将新增接近 40 亿美元的年化营收，⽆需额外
资本⽀出。若将 2028 年续约纳⼊计算，年化增量营收将接近 100 亿美元。
此外，如果整个⾏业的数据中⼼建设放缓，已安装算⼒将变得更加稀缺， GPU 定价可能维持⾼
位的时间也将更⻓。在这种情景下，先发优势⾄关重要。我们已安装的基础设施将在市场上⼤多
数同⾏之前数年完成合同并全额偿清融资。我们在各种市场条件下都处于有利地位：如果⾏业快
速建设，我们随之增⻓；如果供给延迟，我们已偿清的机群将更具价值。我们的计划并不基于这
⼀情景，⽽是建⽴在已签约需求、基于成效的资本⽀出和已经在签的合同之上。
上个季度，我描述了训练、推理、评估和改进如何正在形成⼀个持续的 AI 开发闭环：⽣产中的应

⽤⽣成关于其运⾏效果和不⾜之处的信息，开发者利⽤这些信息对其进⾏改进、测试变更，并将
更好的应⽤投⼊使⽤。这⼀过程需要帮助⼈们从每次实验中学习的复杂软件，以及贯穿应⽤整个
⽣命周期的⾼度可靠的云基础设施。这正是 CoreWeave 最擅⻓的。我们提供⼀个统⼀的平台，
将数⼗万枚 GPU 组合成⼀个⾼性能、全栈云，整合了⽹络、冷却、编排和可观测性能⼒。这就
是客⼾选择 CoreWeave 来处理其最重要⼯作负载的原因。
9 ⽉份，我们推出了 CoreWeave Forge ，将我们的 AI 开发能⼒整合到⼀个统⼀环境中。我们正在
将与领先 AI 实验室共同开发的产品和服务开放给企业客⼾，使他们也能够利⽤⾃⾝专业知识和数
据进⾏构建。第三季度，我们新增的云客⼾中有 70% 已经是 Forge ⽤⼾。
Wave 是我们关系随⼯作深度拓展的⼀个典型案例。他们最初使⽤我们的软件和开发⼯具进⾏实
验和评估模型性能，现在已使⽤ CoreWeave Cloud 来训练其⽤于⾃动驾驶的系列世界模型。
Chan Zuckerberg Initiative 正在利⽤我们的平台开展计算⽣物学研究。 Hippocratic AI 正在训练

和部署智能体，以⽀持患者沟通和医疗导航⼯作，这些努⼒旨在更深⼊地理解疾病，并帮助⼈们
在就医过程中获取所需的信息和⽀持。
在⼯程领域，我们的团队与包括⽇产和阿斯顿 · ⻢丁 · 阿拉姆科⼀级⽅程式⻋队在内的多家机构合
作，致⼒于缩短测试周期、减少达到⽬标精度所需的数据量。物理 AI 的更⼴泛前景是帮助⼯程师
开发出更好的系统，并更快地将有⽤的能⼒带⼊现实世界。 MasterClass 使⽤ Forge 评估其教学
智能体的对话并改善这些体验，这些体验在 CoreWeave Cloud 上运⾏，其⽬标是使学习更能响
应个⼈需求，并在需要时提供帮助。
这 些 客 ⼾ 正 在 从 事 科 学 、 临 床 、 ⼯ 程 和教育 领 域 的 ⼯ 作 ， 将 ⼈ 类 的 探 索 与 A I 相 结 合 。
CoreWeave 为他们提供了⼀个能够以规模和速度追求这些⽬标的平台，这也正是我们的 AI 云的
平台能⼒和基础设施建设都⾄关重要的原因。⼀个有前景的想法与⼀个有⽤的应⽤之间的距离，
在⼀定程度上取决于⼈们能否运⾏实验、评估结果，以及是否负担得起可靠的应⽤服务。

今年，我们将国际扩张列为战略重点。在我们 4.6 吉⽡的签约电⼒中，美国境外已超过 1.3 吉⽡，
涵盖加拿⼤、欧洲和亚洲，均建设在客⼾要求我们驻场的地点。第三季度，我们进⼊了印度尼西
亚和印度等新市场，同时扩⼤了在丹⻨和西班⽛的业务规模。我们的全球 AI ⻣⼲⽹络连接了所有

回报为⽀撑，并从⼴泛多元的来源中汇聚资⾦。将这些要素联系在⼀起，使我们能够根据客⼾需
求、监管格局和资本市场状况来校准投资节奏。

健的同时，基本⾯也在加速改善。我们对⻓期服务客⼾、股东以及合作社区的能⼒充满信⼼。

环境对于拥有差异化技术需求并能够⾼效执⾏的公司⽽⾔极为有利可图。我们基于成效的资本⽀
出模式将投资与客⼾承诺挂钩，我们签订的每⼀份合同都经过核算，以覆盖相关费⽤并产⽣利
润。在每次核算中，我们均纳⼊ GPU 、数据中⼼、电⼒和合同背后的企业管理费⽤，这⼀点从
未改变。

### Page 8

因此，重申我们此前的表态：我们预计来⾃现有客⼾承诺的合同现⾦流将⾜以偿还我们今⽇未偿
债务以及预计为履⾏这些合同⽽筹集的任何额外债务 —— 这既包括资产层⾯的有担保债务，也包
括⺟公司层⾯的⽆担保债务，且这⼀判断尚未计⼊续约收益。
以下进⼊第三季度业绩详述。
营收为 37 亿美元，同⽐增⻓ 168% ，⾼于我们指引区间的上限，反映出 CoreWeave AI 云平台和
服务在强劲执⾏⼒以及有利需求和定价环境下取得的成果。营收积压在本季度末约为 1,330 亿美
元，同⽐增⻓ 138% ，再创历史新⾼。在这笔积压中，超过 50% 与预计在今年年底前开始向客⼾
交付的合同相关联。
运营费⽤为 35 亿美元，其中包括 1.6 亿美元的股权激励费⽤。费⽤增加主要反映了扩⼤活跃电⼒
规模、将积压订单转化为营收的成本，相应推动了营收成本及技术与基础设施⽀出的增加。销售
与市场费⽤持续⽀持我们的市场拓展战略、客⼾群多元化以及多产品采⽤。综合管理费⽤的增加
主要源于⽀撑规模扩张所需的投⼊，但增速持续低于营收增速。

调整后运营收⼊为 3.79 亿美元，调整后运营利润率为 10% ，较 2026 年第⼆季度的 5% 及第⼀季度
的 1% 持续环⽐提升，提前⼀个季度实现了我们年初设定的低两位数利润率⽬标。
利润率超预期表现反映了我们的强劲执⾏⼒、有利的需求和定价环境，以及规模效应带来的经营
杠杆。我们在⻓期和短期合同中均持续看到具有吸引⼒的机会。平台的规模和灵活性使我们能够
在机会出现时，将可⽤容量战略性地分配⾄利润率更⾼的短期合同，这种组合策略对我们第三季
度的强劲业绩贡献显著。
第三季度调整后 EBITDA 为 23 亿美元， 2025 年同期为 8.38 亿美元，同⽐增⻓ 169% ，环⽐增⻓
49% 。调整后 EBITDA 利润率为 62% ，为过去五个季度的最⾼⽔平。这⼀强劲表现转化为第三季
度 20 亿美元的运营现⾦流。随着规模扩⼤，运营现⾦流将持续增⻓，并成为我们增⻓融资中越
来越重要的来源。
第三季度净亏损为 7.09 亿美元， 2025 年同期净亏损为 1.1 亿美元。第三季度利息⽀出为 8.46 亿美

元， 2025 年同期为 3.1 亿美元。调整后运营收⼊ 3.79 亿美元较第⼆季度近乎翻三倍，⽽利息⽀出
仅增加约三分之⼀ —— 这正是通往盈利的路径。调整后净亏损为 5.29 亿美元，⼀年前为 4,100 万
美元。尽管出现净亏损，我们仍计提了所得税拨备，原因是递延所得税资产设置了估值备抵。如
上季度所述，在⽆重⼤特殊项⽬或情况发⽣变化的前提下，我们的税率在 2026 年全年应保持⼤
体稳定。
转向资本⽀出。
第三季度资本⽀出总计 145 亿美元，⾼于我们指引区间的上限，这反映了我们的强劲执⾏⼒使得
部分原定第四季度的交付得以提前。同样的执⾏⼒也推动在建⼯程（ CIP ）从上季度的 119 亿美
元降⾄ 93 亿美元，因为我们针对第⼆季度末新增可⽤电⼒⼤规模部署了基础设施。年初⾄今，
我们已上线超过 850 兆⽡的电⼒，使总活跃规模突破 1.7 吉⽡，仅⽤三个季度就将 2025 年底的活
跃电⼒规模翻了⼀番。这⼀部署速度彰显了我们执⾏模式的实⼒与可复制性，这得益于我们为加
速新容量投产⽽构建的全栈解决⽅案。

转向资产负债表和流动性状况。
截⾄ 9 ⽉ 30 ⽇，我们持有 65 亿美元的现⾦、现⾦等价物、受限制现⾦及有价证券。此外，我们在
已承诺的 DDTL 融资⼯具下还有近 50 亿美元的未提取额度，以及未来三个⽉内将陆续到账的⼤量
客⼾预付款和定⾦。
⾃去年 IPO 以来，我们展现出持续的资本市场融资能⼒，这直接得益于我们将 1,330 亿美元营收
积压转化为现⾦流的⼀贯执⾏记录。仅在未来三个⽉，我们预计就将收到约 90 亿美元的客⼾预

### Page 9

付款和定⾦，且该数字在 2027 年将持续增⻓。如 Mike 所指出，其中超过 30 亿美元与⽆需额外资
本⽀出的现有云基础设施相关。我们预计 2027 年扣除利息后的 EBITDA （不含预付款增⻓带来的
收益）将翻⼀番以上，这将进⼀步提升我们内部满⾜资本需求的能⼒。我们预计资产层⾯的债务
将覆盖其余资本需求的绝⼤部分。
我们的资产负债表上存在两种本质不同的债务：⼀是资产层⾯的债务，直接为有承诺现⾦流和云
基础设施⽀撑的客⼾合同提供融资；⼆是⺟公司层⾯的债务，由⺟公司现⾦流提供偿债服务。过
去⼀年，我们使⽤⺟公司层⾯的⽆担保债务协助为增⻓提供资⾦，因为这些⼯具在当时的定价优
化了我们的整体经济效益。如 Mike 所述，以当前收益率⽽⾔，我们不打算在 2027 年之前增发⺟
公司层⾯的⽆担保债务。相⽐之下，资产层⾯的有担保债务市场保持稳定，我们的贷款继续在⾯
值附近交易。贷款⽅对我们在履⾏已承诺客⼾合同过程中产⽣的现⾦流具有直接的可⻅性，该市
场仍然健康，且⾃ IPO 以来，该市场对 CoreWeave 的深度持续增加。

实际上，我们预计在未来数周内启动下⼀笔 DDTL ，这将是⼀个投资级融资⼯具，多家评级机构
已初步表明其信⽤评级处于较⾼的投资级⽔平。根据我们的早期洽谈，预计该融资⼯具按当前
SOFR 利率的隐含收益率为 7% ⾄ 7.5% ，同时覆盖超过 90% 的相关资本⽀出。在尚未纳⼊现有
DDTL 的 2027 年剩余承诺资本⽀出中，逾 75% 与上述类型的投资级和企业客⼾挂钩。对于积压订
单的其余部分，我们预计将利⽤结构类似的 DDTL ，并以客⼾预付款和 OEM 融资作为⽀持增⻓的
其他主要资本来源。
在已承诺的客⼾积压之外，我们还看到了其他可利⽤的途径。 CoreWeave Omni 提供了⼀个以显
著更低资本密集度实现增⻓的额外选项。通过将 CoreWeave 平台延伸⾄第三⽅基础设施， Omni
使我们能够在不⾃⾏出资购买底层资产的情况下，将增量客⼾需求货币化。我们已签约⾸位客
⼾，并收到⼤量其他客⼾的浓厚兴趣 —— 这些客⼾希望在维持其基础设施直接所有权的同时，在
CoreWeave 上运⾏其⼯作负载。这是我们⼯具箱中的⼜⼀有效⼿段，使我们能够根据当前环境

在资本需求与客⼾需求之间取得平衡。
转向指引。
我们强劲的第三季度表现和持续的执⾏⼒，使我们有信⼼将 2026 年全年营收指引上调⾄ 130 亿⾄
133 亿美元，调整后运营收⼊指引上调⾄ 12.75 亿⾄ 13.75 亿美元，中值较此前增⻓ 26% 。我们继
续预计年底活跃电⼒超过 1.85 吉⽡。预计资本⽀出为 370 亿⾄ 390 亿美元，将此前 370 亿⾄ 390 亿
美元区间的下限从 350 亿美元上调，这是由于我们预计将⽐计划更早地部署基础设施并向客⼾交
付。第四季度预计利息⽀出为 10.5 亿⾄ 11.5 亿美元。
基于我们预计部署的容量以及积压订单中蕴含的合同营收，我们上调 ARR 指引，预计以 200 亿⾄
210 亿美元的年化运⾏率营收结束 2026 年。展望未来，我们预计调整后净亏损在 2027 年将⼤幅
收窄，并在 2028 年上半年及全年实现调整后净利润转正。
就 2027 年⽽⾔，我们在执⾏增⻓计划的速度上保留灵活性。我们仅在需求旺盛、能够以具有吸
引⼒的条款获取融资且预期回报达到我们⻔槛的情况下才会部署资本。随着市场条件演变，我们

可相应调整未来的资本承诺，这不会影响我们履⾏现有客⼾承诺，也不会影响我们⼤幅超额完成
2027 年 ARR ⽬标的能⼒。
这意味着我们拥有控制资本⽀出的杠杆。如果我们选择仅专注于已承诺的积压订单，预计 2027
年资本⽀出将与 2026 年持平，同时营收和 EBITDA 将显著增⻓。为交付这些容量，我们已在已筹
集的 DDTL 下可提取近 50 亿美元，并有超过 90 亿美元的所需基础设施已在资产负债表上以在建⼯
程的形式存在，等待投⼊使⽤。我们的⽀出还得到以下来源的⽀持：预计在未来三个⽉从已签署
客⼾合同中收取的约 90 亿美元预付款和定⾦、 EBITDA 产⽣的现⾦流增⻓，以及我们预计筹集的
额外 DDTL 和资产⽀持融资⼯具（包括我前述的近期交易）。综合以上来源，我们预计 2027 年之
前在⺟公司层⾯筹集的增量资本不⾜ 50 亿美元。

### Page 10

简⽽⾔之，即便在当前的宏观经济条件下，我们对为业务增⻓提供融资的能⼒充满信⼼。本季度
的表现源于我们数年前在如何建设和融资这项业务⽅⾯所做出的深思熟虑的决策，以及对具有吸
引⼒回报的追求。我们构建了专为 AI 打造的差异化技术，培养了以速度和规模快速部署⾼性能云
基础设施的运营能⼒，并设计了基于成效的资本⽀出模式，将投资与已签约需求挂钩，同时拥有
多元化的资本来源，确保 CoreWeave 能够以纪律和合理成本为其增⻓提供融资。
我们对 AI 建设中所承担的⻆⾊极为认真。这是⼀个资本密集型业务，⽽我们是这些资本的受托管
理者。我们针对已承诺客⼾合同以⽬标贡献利润率进⾏盈利性规模化部署，并能够根据市场条件
加速或放缓这⼀节奏。这种纪律将持续指引我们的增⻓⽅式。前⽅的机遇⽆限⼴阔 ——AI 正在改
变世界的建设、探索与创造⽅式，⽽ CoreWeave 正是为⽀撑这场变⾰⽽⽣。

出，尚未成交的部分也有⼤量需求可供承接。

⼾合同带来的可预测现⾦流、⾼端产品（如托管推理等更⾼层次服务）带来的溢价，以及短期客
⼾合同。在管理这三者时，我们逐项评估每⼀个机会 —— 理解其将产⽣的回报、融资成本以及各
⽅向的需求环境，以此进⾏内部容量分配。
在回报⽅⾯，短期合同的资本⽀出回收期要快得多，⼤约在 6 ⾄ 18 个⽉；⽽在当前定价⽔平下，
硬件资本⽀出的⻓期客⼾合同回收期⼤约在 1 ⾄ 2 年。
Michael Intrator ，⾸席执⾏官：  还有⼀点⾮常有意思：随着初始合同到期，如我们此前所说，
2027 年将有 13 万枚 GPU 结束合同， 2028 年还有约 17 万枚到期。这些 GPU 将为我们提供极佳的

### Page 14

⻛险提⽰及免责条款
市场有⻛险，投资需谨慎。本⽂不构成个⼈投资建议，也未考虑到个别⽤⼾特殊的投资⽬标、财务状况或需要。⽤⼾应考虑本⽂
中的任何意⻅、观点或结论是否符合其特定状况。据此投资，责任⾃负。

短期合同变现机会。值得特别说明的是，这些基础设施在初始合同期末均已全额偿清，这正是延
迟提款定期贷款（ DDTL ）的设计逻辑，这使我们能够对现⾦流产⽣实质性的正⾯影响。
Amit Daryanani ，分析师：  明⽩。再追问⼀下这个话题：您提到 2027 年续约带来近 40 亿美元
增量营收， 2028 年增⾄ 100 亿美元。请问这是总营收贡献还是增量提升？这些是否已签约，还是
预期中的续约？这些数字看起来⾮常强劲，对应的 EBITDA 利润率也应相当可观。
Nitin Agrawal ，⾸席财务官：  这些数字在您⽬前看到的营收积压之外，因为积压反映的是当前
已签约容量。当我们对这些容量进⾏续约时，这代表了按当前定价⽔平针对该基础设施可产⽣的

ARR 机遇 —— ⽽这些基础设施均为已部署的基础设施，⽆需增量资本⽀出。
需要特别说明的是，在 90 亿美元的预付款定⾦中，超过 30 亿美元是针对已全额偿清的现有基础
设施的预付款。这意味着这 30 亿美元进⼊公司账⼾时，完全不需要任何债务偿还，且对应的基
础设施已经历了 DDTL 和初始合同的完整周期，这正是它如此强⼤的原因所在。

## Document 355: Europe’s Deal Limiting Chinese Auto Imports (2026-11-02)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Europe’s Deal Limiting Chinese Auto Imports (2026-11-02).pdf
Report source: CFR (Follow the Money)
Date: 2026-11-02
Pages: 16
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

1
EXPERT TAKE FOLLOW THE MONEY
Europe’s Deal Limiting Chinese Auto Imports
Europe’s auto deal with China is a good start. But containing China’s export boom
will require a much broader response.
European Commissioner for Trade and Economic Security Maros Sefcovic speaks at a press briefing at the EU Delegation to
China in Beijing, China October 9, 2026. Florence Lo/Reuters
PUBLISHED
Articles Follow the Money Share
Log In
Regions Topics Research Experts Events The Future of American Strategy

### Page 2

2
November 2, 2026 11:02 a.m.
Brad W. Setser CFR EXPERT
Whitney Shepardson Senior Fellow
The year-over-year increase in Chinese automobile
exports to all markets is currently about 3.5 million
cars.
Over ten years, that is 35 million cars. The global
market excluding China is only around 60 million
cars. Japan raised its auto exports by about 3.5 million
cars in the 1970s—but that was over a decade, not
one year. And right now the U.S. and Indian markets

(around 20 million cars) are effectively closed to
Chinese production. The 2026 jump in exports is an
incredible.
China’s domestic market, though, is down 20 percent
this year—that works out to a decline of about 5
million cars. The Wall Street Journal estimates that
domestic sales will fall to around 19 million cars this
year, less than a third of the production capacity of
China’s auto industry.
So even though China’s exports are increasing at an

unprecedented pace, China’s domestic auto sector
isn’t really booming; production is essentially flat, and
intense domestic competition is destroying the
profitability of firms that don’t export.*

### Page 3

3
Europe’s new auto deal with China should be
understood in this context.
The deal limits China’s exports of hybrids (including
plug-in hybrids) to Europe to around 350,000 cars.
That is above the 300,000 hybrids that China
exported to Europe in 2025, but well below the (gulp)
800,000 annualized pace for hybrids in the last three
months of (European) data. Total imports,
annualized, were running at an incredible 2 million a
year pace thanks to strong battery electric imports in

September.

### Page 4

4
China appears to have concluded that it prefers a
guaranteed market for a limited number of cars (and
more or less guaranteed profits) to the risk of a
prohibitive tariff that would cut off all its exports.
And Europe preferred a negotiated deal to the risk of
retaliation and a slowdown in licensing rare earth and
magnet exports.
The Commission also thinks of this as a bit of a
model—more on that later.
I certainly understand the logic of the deal.

Something needed to be done. China’s export of
hybrids to Europe were up an insane 200 percent
year-over-year in the last 3 months of data, which led
to a 75 percent increase for all cars. One more year of
75 percent growth would bring China’s exports to

### Page 5

5
around 3 million cars; in two years, close to 6 million,
and in three years, 10 million—or most of the market.
But Europe still is a long way from a coherent trade
and industrial policy with respect to autos, let alone a
coherent economic policy towards overall trade with
China.
After this deal, as I understand it:
Hybrid imports are capped at 350,000, down
from a projected 800,000 in 2026—and then
rising to 370,000 and 390,000 in subsequent
years.

Battery electric vehicles face a tariff from 25
percent (Telsa, 10 percent base + 15 percent) to
45 percent (10 percent base plus 35 percent).
BYD’s total tariff is at 27 percent, which isn’t
prohibitive. Even with these tariffs, battery
electric imports will total around 600,000 this
year, up from 500,000 last year.
Diesel and gasoline (ICE) cars face a tariff of
10 percent. Europe is importing around
200,000 of these a year (including a significant

number from Chinese-owned MG).
There are no special tariffs on imported parts or
imported batteries, which matters for the growing
number of Chinese factories in Europe. So long as
tariffs are low and the renminbi is undervalued,
Chinese firms still have an incentive to maximize the

### Page 6

6
Chinese content of their “transplant” factories (ask
Thailand).
There is currently no unified European policy on
inward investment that requires joint ventures or
technology transfer either, only a lot of wishful
thinking.**
Sum it up, and it is a market where Chinese exports
are constrained by a hodge-podge of different trade
defense tools, and there is no standard policy for
ensuring that Chinese assembly operations use
European parts rather than import Chinese kits. The

existing operations, which are just starting to scale up,
don’t draw on a European supply chain. They likely
preserve a few assembly jobs at the expense of the
broader automotive ecosystem.
A coherent European (or national) industrial policy
for the automotive and battery sectors is thus still a
work in progress.
The safeguard on hybrids and the resulting tariff rate
quota only stops the bleeding.
And make no mistake, Europe’s position in the global

automotive sector is slipping. Europe still runs an
overall surplus in auto trade, but that surplus is falling
fast if trade in batteries is added to automotive trade
(there isn’t a split between batteries used for energy

### Page 7

7
storage and batteries used by cars in the EU data
right now, at least not that I have found).
Europe’s broader trade policy to China is similarly a
work in progress.
The surge in imports of hybrid cars was particularly
pronounced, but it is by no means the only such surge.
The balance in industrial machinery has eroded
significantly. Consider the following chart, which
shows imports and exports of machinery, electronics
and vehicles (HS 84, 85, and 87) by weight.

### Page 8

8
The balance in chemicals, measured by weight to
abstract from price changes, shows the same trend.
Europe doesn’t have an across-the-board tool for
applying tariffs against China like the U.S. section
301 statute.

### Page 9

9
But that isn’t necessarily an immediate constraint, as
there are plenty of solid safeguard cases thanks to the
current surge in European imports from China.
But if each safeguard needs to include a negotiated
tariff-rate quota that gives China enough guaranteed
market access (and Chinese firms enough guaranteed
profits) to ensure trade peace, Europe needs to get
moving with negotiation in other sectors.
Chemicals and machinery are more diverse sectors

than cars and thus aren’t as amenable to the same
high-level Š ef č ovi č -Wang negotiations the led to the
most recent deal.
This critique isn’t meant as criticism. Europe needed
to act on cars. Look at a chart of automotive trade in
kilos.

### Page 10

10
It’s just that it also needs to act in other sectors: auto
parts, batteries (though that is complicated),
chemicals, and some categories of industrial
machinery. The scale of China’s export boom and the
fact that in most sectors there are no limits to China’s
ability to scale up is a real problem.***
And Europe cannot wait for China to conclude that
it would rather grow on the basis of internal, rather
than external, demand.
I disagree with the IMF’s former top brass on this. A

China that grows on the back of a 1.5 pp net export
contribution for another couple of years will
deindustrialize much of the world. China’s trading
partners cannot step aside and do nothing until
China figures out how to escape deflation using its
own demand. Xi appears more than happy to
continue with his current policy mix and gamble that

### Page 11

11
the world will continue to absorb China’s ever
growing export surplus.
Pascal Lamy, Nicolas Köhler-Suzuki, and Victor Luiz
do Prado of the Jacques Delors Institute recognize
the need for a more systemic response—as do Jean-
Pierre Landau and Sébastien Jean, who have done an
excellent job explaining how China’s micro- and
macro-industrial policies fit together into a coherent,
self-reinforcing whole.
The proposals in the Lamy et al. paper overlap heavily

with the Commission’s current approach to China.
The authors envision a two-pronged approach: first,
negotiating with China to seek agreement on tariff-
rate quotas that effectively limit the EU’s imports
from China; and second, developing a broad yet
targeted tariff-based instrument to address structural
imbalances should negotiations fail. The latter would
give Europe a credible threat should China refuse to
agree to the proposed safeguards.
My own view: don’t let the perfect be the enemy of

the good.
The EU has shied away from developing its own 301
tool out of concern about preserving a “rules-based
system.” I still think a European 301 would allow a
more fulsome response, but understand that
European isn’t as willing as the United States to use

### Page 12

12
tools that at least appear to be outside those
authorized by the WTO. In reality the EU no longer
really wants to trade with China on the same (MFN)
terms applied to other WTO members. That mirrors
the current view of the United States. But the EU is
willing to go through more procedural hoops to get to
a similar result.****
But I also recognize that, absent policy adjustment in
China—notably a stronger currency that allows for
fair market competition—the scope of safeguards and

managed trade will progressively expand.
* One sign: Stellantis is resuming Jeep production in
China, but primarily for export rather than the
domestic market.
** There is a lot of (in my view) somewhat confused
analysis of Chinese investment that works off the
analogy provided by either foreign investment in
China’s auto sector or Japanese investment in the U.S.
and Europe in the 1980s. These are relevant examples
of course, but asserting that Chinese investment will

result in a similar outcome when the underlying
economics differ is a big logical jump. Japanese firms
had incentives to localize their parts production in the
United States from the then-strong yen as well as
from the risk of protection. Foreign firms operating in
China had to do JVs until recently and had incentives
to localize from China’s significant tariff on auto

### Page 13

13
parts and China’s lower cost structure (in part because
of a weak RMB). Tesla’s Shanghai incentives and tax
deal included strong localization incentives, and there
were additional incentives from Tesla’s desire to
qualify for the lists the governed access to China’s
consumer EV subsidies. That is why something like
90 percent of Tesla parts, including its batteries, are
now made in China. Chinese investors in Europe
have no similar incentives to localize right now, yet

many assume that the results will be the same. That,
to me, is naïve. Since the underlying economics differ
(Europe’s cost basis is much higher than existing
production in China) getting Chinese firms to do
more than final assembly in Europe will take stronger
policy tools than were required to get foreign firms to
localize parts production in China. Europe’s
Industrial Accelerator Act would be a step in the
right direction, but more is likely needed. Thailand

provides a useful cautionary example.
*** China can make over 60 million cars, according to
recent reporting from the Wall Street Journal. The
domestic market this year will be under 20 million
cars (down from something like 24 million cars last
year). Even with exports of 10 million, there is ~30
million cars worth of unused auto production
capacity (roughly double European demand). And
China is still investing at a pace that is consistent
with adding close to 5 million of EV production

capacity a year. Chinese firms, who don’t make any
money on domestic sales, are strongly incentivized to

### Page 14

14
This work represents the views solely of the author(s). The
Council on Foreign Relations is an independent,
nonpartisan membership organization, think tank, and
publisher, and takes no institutional positions on matters
of policy.
From Follow the Money and Greenberg Center for Geoeconomics
export. The world has never encountered such elastic
supply.
**** The more different the legal basis for the
European and U.S. response—and the more policy
differences (TRQs vs. a flat tariff, for example) the

harder it would be to ever develop a harmonized
policy approach. But with President Trump, a
harmonized policy isn’t even a U.S. goal.

### Page 15

## Document 370: Global Views_ Downside Growth Risks

Source PDF: all report pdf/Global Views_ Downside Growth Risks.pdf
Report source: Goldman Sachs Publishing
Date: 2026-10-26
Pages: 10
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Jan Hatzius 
+1(212)902-0394 | jan.hatzius@gs.com 
Goldman Sachs & Co. LLC
Global Views: Downside Growth Risks
26 October 2026 | 6:45AM EDT
1. So far in 2026, global growth has been remarkably unremarkable.  Both the world 
as a whole and the G3 economies are growing in line with our estimates of potential 
GDP .  Our interpretation is that fiscal easing and the positive impulse from the AI 
boom have offset the negative impulse from the war in Iran and the lingering drag 
from higher US tariffs.

2. Our standing global GDP forecasts project more of the same in 2027, as lower 
energy prices offset fading tailwinds from fiscal policy and AI.  But the balance of 
risks is on the downside.  The most immediate concern is that, far from subsiding, 
energy prices have again climbed sharply on fears of renewed Middle East escalation.  
The prices of gasoline, diesel, jet fuel, and European/Asian natural gas are now near 
or even above their March/April highs, with likely negative effects on consumers and 
energy-importing economies more broadly.

### Page 2

3. Although we don’t expect significant fiscal policy changes in 2027, the consequences 
of large-scale deficits—coupled with AI-linked borrowing and central bank hikes—have 
started to show up more clearly in higher longer-term interest rates.  The moves have 
been mostly orderly, but the increase since early 2026 averages a sizable 100bp across 
DM yield curves.  If sustained, we estimate that this increase will subtract a cumulative 
0.6% from GDP via weakness in housing, consumer durables, and business investment.  
(Only part of the drag is incorporated in our standing forecasts because we expect bond 
yields to pull back as central banks deliver fewer hikes than priced in markets.)

Exhibit 2: Oil Price Risks Are Skewed to the Upside

Source: Goldman Sachs Global Investment Research
26 October 2026   2
Goldman Sachs Global Views

### Page 3

4. The growth impulse from the AI boom was set to peak in late 2026 or early 2027 even 
before concerns around the sustainability of the AI investment boom intensified in 
recent weeks.  Although AI investment is still rising rapidly, we expect the increase as a 
share of GDP to slow over the next year.  Alongside the fading boost from last year’s 
increase in US depreciation allowances, this will weigh on capital spending growth, 
especially in the US.  AI has also boosted US consumer spending growth via a positive 
wealth effect worth an estimated 0.4pp (or 0.25% of GDP) over the past year.  This 
impact is likely to decline over the next year, gradually if stocks perform in line with our 
strategists’ reasonably upbeat expectations but more sharply if AI-related stocks 
continue to decline.

### Page 4

5. Our own base case remains that AI investment will ultimately pay off in terms of higher 
productivity and economywide profits.  However, one key channel for this payoff is labor 
market disruption, which has negative near-term macroeconomic effects.  And while DM 
labor markets still look solid in aggregate, we are starting to see clearer signs that the AI 
impact is spreading beyond the technology sector.  Across nonmanufacturing industries, 
the share of US firms that have adopted AI in regular business functions now shows a 
statistically significant negative correlation with employment growth and a statistically 
significant positive correlation with unemployment.

6. The Fed’s recent signals—explicit from Williams/Jefferson and implicit from Warsh—
effectively rule out a hike on October 28.  But the FOMC still looks very likely to deliver 
another 25bp hike to 4-4.25% on December 9 because spot inflation remains too high.  
And while we continue to expect significant disinflation next year on the back of 
reduced impulses from tariffs, energy prices, and AI-related prices, the risks to our 
forecast are on the upside, most obviously in energy.  Higher inflation could result in

another hike in March, but either way we see renewed cuts in late 2027/early 2028.

7. Like the Fed, the ECB will likely hold rates steady next week and deliver a final hike (to 
2.75%) at the December meeting.  However, the turbulence in the French bond market 
poses somewhat greater downside risks to that call than in the US.  The turbulence has 
tightened financial conditions to a degree that is likely to shave ¼-½pp from annualized 
growth in coming quarters.  And if it intensifies into December, markets might perceive a 
hike as signaling insufficient focus by the ECB on the risk of Euro area fragmentation.  
While the ECB could address this issue by coupling a hike with renewed balance sheet 
expansion, the hurdle for such a move is high, in part because purchases of French 
bonds would inevitably raise concerns about moral hazard with respect to the needed 
fiscal adjustment.

November 5.  Consistent with our view that the UK economy is stronger than widely 
perceived, GDP growth has surprised on the upside this year and the unemployment rate 
declined to 4.7% in August on a 3-month average basis (with the volatile 1-month rate 
plunging to 4.1%).  Rising inflation and the looming household energy price reset in 
January imply a significant risk of further moves, although market pricing of 4+ hikes 
(including November) still looks excessive.  We also expect hikes in Canada and New 
Zealand this week and in Australia next week.

9. Slightly faster than expected growth in Q3 has pushed up our 2026 GDP forecast for 
China to 4.6%.  But most activity indicators are much weaker.  Both property activity and 
fixed asset investment are contracting year-on-year, and while nominal retail sales 
growth picked up to +1.1% in September, large retailers (whose sales are relatively 
better measured) are still posting substantial year-on-year declines.  With domestic 
demand rising slowly at best, sustained GDP growth in the official 4.5-5% target range 
will require ongoing steep increases in the trade surplus.  But the surplus is already at 1% 
of global GDP , and further increases will eat more noticeably into output and 
employment abroad.  Ultimately, China’s trading partners may respond by imposing 
trade barriers that weigh on Chinese exports and GDP , although the measures taken so 
far have been limited.

## Document 377: India_ A middle class too small to supplant China’s economic role (2026-10-26)

Source PDF: all report pdf/India_ A middle class too small to supplant China’s economic role (2026-10-26).pdf
Report source: BNP Paribas
Date: 2026-10-26
Pages: 8
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

The bank
for a changing
world
At a time when Chinese consumption is slowing significantly, could India become a driver of global consumption? For now, this
prospect presents considerable difficulties, with many obstacles to overcome. Despite dynamic growth and the Indian economy’s
expanding share of global output (8.2% of worldwide GDP in 2025), the middle-class consumer population remains modest and is nowhere
near the size of China’s.
This is due to the concentration of employment in low-value-added and largely informal sectors. The few manufacturing sectors with

high value-added and strong job potential — electrical equipment, electronics, automotive, chemicals and pharmaceuticals — continue to
face constraints from an unfavourable business environment and a skills shortage that is holding back their move into premium market
segments.
Furthermore, the highest-paying service sector jobs, particularly in information technology, are now threatened by the rise of
artificial intelligence. Without concrete measures, the “demographic window” — which is expected to remain favourable for another 15 to

25 years — could close before India establishes a solid industrial base and a sufficiently large middle class.
INDIA: A MIDDLE CLASS TOO SMALL TO SUPPLANT CHINA’S ECONOMIC ROLE
Johanna Melka
issue 20
26 October 2026

economic-research.bnpparibas.comEcoInsight 20 / 26 October 2026
The bank
for a changing
worldINDIA: A MIDDLE CLASS TOO SMALL TO SUPPLANT CHINA’S ECONOMIC ROLE
INDIA IS STRUGGLING TO ESTABLISH A ROBUST MIDDLE
CLASS
With an annual growth rate of 7.3% over the past decade (excluding the
pandemic), India has one of the highest growth rates among the world’s
major economies. Yet, its middle-class consumer base remains surpri-
singly small. When measured in current U.S. dollars, total consumption

is very high in India (USD 2,241 billion in 2025), but this figure prima -
rily reflects the country’s overall population size. By comparison, this
consumption level falls 3.5 times short of China’s corresponding figure.
Furthermore, per capita consumption adjusted for purchasing power
parity (PPP) is 1.3 times lower than that of Indonesia, the Philippines
and Vietnam. When evaluated at constant prices, consumption per ca-
pita has increased only slightly over the past thirty years compared to

China and even Vietnam (Chart 1).
According to our estimates, based on the research conducted by Ló -
pez-Calva and Ortiz-Juarez (2014) 1, India’s middle-class consumer
segment accounts for just 1.6% of the total population (see Table 1),
or fewer than 24 million people. This is less than in Thailand and Viet-
nam (more than 30 million each) and far below China (approximately
490 million, according to the same definition).
The majority of households identified as middle class by standard In -

dian research institutions (the Centre for Monitoring Indian Economy
[CMIE] and the National Council of Applied Economic Research [NCAER])
would, in fact, be categorised as “vulnerable” according to World Bank
criteria: households that have officially emerged from poverty yet lack
the financial stability necessary for sustained economic security.
Highly concentrated consumption and limited
upward mobility
The structure of Indian household consumption reveals significant

disparities between urban and rural areas. In 2024, average month -
ly consumption per capita amounted to 6,996 rupees in urban areas
1 These authors define the middle class as individuals for whom the risk of falling back into poverty is limited (less than 4% over four years), corresponding to daily per capita income
ranging from 17 to 98 2021 PPP dollars (with vulnerability thresholds at 6.85 and 14 2017 PPP dollars, according to the World Bank). In the absence of reliable national income

data collection mechanisms in India, our study applies these thresholds to the World Bank’s consumer expenditure data, an approach aligned with the objective of assessing actual
purchasing capacity deployed in the market (although this tends to slightly underestimate the middle class in terms of income, as household savings remain unaccounted for in
expenditure measurements).
(approximately USD 84.5), compared with 4,122 rupees in rural areas
(less than USD 50). The wealthiest 5% of urban consumers spent an

average equivalent of USD 245 per month per person. Consequently,
discretionary spending power remained concentrated among the hi -
ghest income brackets.
The structure of consumption has changed over the past decade. The
proportion allocated to food purchases (34.8% of total spending on ave-
rage) declined by 4.3 percentage points between 2011–12 and 2023–
24, while allocations to transportation and communications increased
to 12.4% (+3.8 pp). Although the allocation to durable goods has in -

creased, it remains below 7% of total household spending. This increase
was driven more by a rise in the use of credit than by income growth.
Over the past ten years, consumer loans have been the main source of
household debt. Although still modest relative to GDP , household debt
rose by 12.2 pp to reach 47.8% of GDP by the end of 2025, according to
the Bank for International Settlements (BIS).

THE LABOUR MARKET SUFFERS FROM STRUCTURAL
WEAKNESSES
The limited size of the middle class and the low level of household
consumption in India can be attributed, in particular, to the enduring
structural weaknesses of the labour market. The labour market faces
two major problems: insufficient job creation and the fact that most of
the jobs created are low-skilled and low-paid.

Insufficient job creation
Despite the Indian economy’s robust growth, its employment rate re -
mains substantially lower than in comparable Asian economies: 52.7%
in 2025 according to the International Labour Organization (ILO), com-
pared with 65.6% in Indonesia and 71.3% in Vietnam. This disparity
is mainly due to low labour force participation among women (34%
compared with 53.4% and 68%) and young people (40.8% of those aged
20-24 compared with 66.7% and 69.4%).

Over the past ten years, employment growth has averaged just 2.4%
annually, whereas GDP growth was close to 6% per year.
The services sector (representing 33.2% of employment in 2025) ac -
counted for the bulk of job creation during this period (+3.2% per year),
far ahead of the manufacturing sector (+1.4%) and, above all, agricultu-
re (+0.1%), which nevertheless remains the country’s largest employer
(41.5% of the workforce). This is not enough to absorb the 10 to 12 mil-

lion new entrants to the labour market each year.
2 Employment elasticity remained relatively stable between 1990 and 2012.
A limited number of sectors have significant po-
tential for creating high-quality, well-paid jobs
To identify sectors with high potential for job creation, we calculate
employment elasticity both for aggregate economy (KLEMS data) and
for the formal manufacturing sector alone (Annual Survey of Industries,
2025).
Over the period 1981–2023, aggregate employment elasticity remained

low at a virtually unchanged level, close to 0.3. This stability, however,
masks significant sectoral divergences: elasticity remained stable in
the services sector throughout the period (0.5), but it declined shar -
ply in the manufacturing sector (from 0.4 during 1981–1990 to just
0.1 during 2012–2023 2): at the same growth rate, India now creates
far fewer industrial jobs per unit of growth than it did forty years ago
(Table 2). In the formal manufacturing sector alone, employment elas-

ticity reached 0.7 over the past ten years, driven by very robust job
creation. However, this positive trend has not been sufficient to offset
the decline in informal sector employment, which accounts for 73.4%
of total manufacturing employment but only 38% of the sector’s value
added. Labour productivity in the formal manufacturing sector is thus
estimated to be 5.4 times higher than in the informal sector.
In capital-intensive sectors, employment elasticity ranged from 0.5 to

0.6 (including electrical equipment, transportation, chemicals and me-
tals), whereas in labour-intensive sectors — specifically wood, textiles
and apparel, and manufacturing food — employment elasticities re -
mained particularly low, or in certain cases, negative.

In other words, growth in value added in these sectors with high labour 
absorption potential is primarily driven by productivity gains rather 
than job creation. Furthermore, even among companies operating in 
the formal sector, employment elasticity remains low at an average of 
0.4. Consequently, the traditional mechanism facilitating labour mobi-
lity from the agricultural sector to the labour-intensive manufacturing 
sector has been very limited over the past forty years.
The only manufacturing subsectors that combine strong growth, high 
employment elasticity, and high productivity — thereby offering real 
potential for creating high-quality jobs — are electronics and infor -
mation technology, electrical equipment, the automotive industry, the 
chemical industry, and, to a lesser extent, the pharmaceutical industry. 
In these subsectors, the formal employment elasticity ranges from 0.8 
to 1.2 (Chart 2). However, in 2025, these jobs accounted for just 1.4% 
of total employment and 5.9% of GDP . The potential therefore remains 
negligible at the macroeconomic level.
Several structural constraints limit job creation in 
India
- The high rigidity of the labour market: it consists mainly of small, in-
formal businesses — those with fewer than 50 employees account for 
65% of the productive sector — largely due to the stringency of labour 
legislation (for example, companies with more than 300 employees 
cannot carry out mass layoffs without authorisation).
- Numerous obstacles impede the growth of industrial and job-crea -
ting businesses: an unfavourable business environment (particularly 
difficulties in accessing land acquisition to build factories); high costs 
of accessing capital (particularly for small and medium-sized enter -
prises); insufficient export competitiveness in labour-intensive sectors 
(textiles, clothing), which encounter disadvantages, including less ad -
vantageous tariff conditions3 than competing Asian economies.
- The mismatch between the skills of the available workforce and the 
needs of businesses. This mismatch constrains wage growth and the 
development of household purchasing power, obstructs industrial ad -
vancement, and, consequently, the creation of high-quality jobs.
INADEQUATE JOB QUALITY DUE TO A LACK OF  
APPROPRIATE SKILLS
A predominantly informal and low-paid labour  
market
According to the ILO, 87.2% of total employment in India is informal, 
with 73.4% concentrated in the manufacturing sector alone, predomi -
nantly engaged in low-value-added activities that require few skills. 
Furthermore, the Periodic Labour Force Survey (PLFS) 2023/24 indi -
cates that 58.4% of Indian workers are self-employed, 19.8% are day 
labourers, and only 21.7% are regular employees — the highest-paying 
status, with a wage gap of more than 60% compared to the other two 
categories. According to the ILO, 20% of the population captures 79% of 
aggregate labour income. The agricultural sector, which still employs 
41.5% of the labour force while contributing less than 15% to GDP , is

3 Historically, tariff rates imposed on Indian products exported to Europe have been higher than those applied to certain other Asian countries (Bangladesh, Vietnam, Pakistan).
4 According to the World Bank and the NCAER, educational and training programmes are too theoretical and fail to develop cognitive and interpersonal skills. Vocational education 
and training are not designed to meet the needs of high-demand sectors. Collaborative relationships between educational institutions and employers — particularly regarding 
internships and on-the-job training — remain inadequately developed.
3.7 times less productive than the services sector, with a median hourly 
wage that is 40 to 50% lower.
A widespread skills shortage
This concentration of earnings reflects the low skill levels of the jobs 
held: according to the PLFS, 88.2% of workers hold jobs requiring little 
or no skill. The proportion of skilled jobs is just 11.2% and has declined 
by 4.6 pp since 2018. The underlying challenge is not a shortage of 
graduates but rather a misalignment between what the labour market 
needs and the educational preparation provided to graduates 4, which 
results in their gradual downward mobility into positions far below 
their formal qualifications.
Unemployment, the downward mobility of graduates, and a shortage 
of technical skills
This disparity between skills and business needs directly contributes to 
unemployment among graduates, which reached 13.5% in 2025 accor-
ding to the ILO, compared with 1.3% for individuals without a degree. 
Within the 25- to 34-year-old demographic, the unemployment rate 
stands at 15.2% for graduates (19.2% for those with a master’s de -
gree), compared with an average of 6.1% across this age group. Only 
8.3% of graduates secure positions that genuinely correspond to their 
educational attainment; 53% of bachelor’s degree holders and 36% of 
master’s degree holders are overqualified for their positions.
In low-skilled jobs, the lack of technical training represents a signifi -
cant barrier to youth employment: according to the World Bank’s En -
terprise Survey (2022), 17.8% of traditional service companies and 14% 
of “intermediate” manufacturing companies cite this as a major obsta-
cle. In India’s manufacturing sector, 32.9% of positions are reportedly 
held by underqualified workers, according to the ILO, compared with 
28.8% in Indonesia and just 16.5% in Vietnam.
A direct impediment to industrial advancement
A survey conducted by the Ministry of Skill Development & Entrepre -
neurship across seven key sectors — automotive, textiles, agriculture, 
livestock, IT, commerce and green energy — reveals that the pace of
